| Precedence | Authority for product behaviour, journeys and copy (`docs/01-DECISIONS.md` §0). Subordinate to `docs/00-GOAL.md`, `docs/01-DECISIONS.md` and, for priority and 1.0 scope, `docs/03-REQUIREMENTS.md`. Numbers and algorithms defer to `docs/accounting/ENGINE_SPEC.md` (with `ENGINE_SPEC_B.md`, D-74), `docs/accounting/POLICIES.md` and the answer keys; tables and API shapes defer to `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` and `docs/05-ARCHITECTURE.md`; visuals defer to `docs/design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` and SCREENS (`docs/design/SCREENS.md` with `docs/design/SCREENS_B.md`, D-74). **Identifier authority (D-73):** enumeration literals (04 §3), permission codes (04 T-PLT-11), problem slugs (04 §15.2) and finding and exception codes (04 §15.4) are owned by `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` and cited here verbatim; registry keys and option literals are owned by POLICIES §1 (D-13a); route paths are owned by SCREENS, while SF ids stay stable here. UI labels are copy. |

With the item the contracts the changed rates reach are recalculated, and what the rates change in the amounts of their events is posted with an origin period and listed in the out-of-period register; and a closing rate corrected in a period that is followed by another closed period leaves nothing to post for a balance open through both. The last part of the message is restated to say the three; `<posting>` is the period what remains of the difference posts to. The copy row is registry-held (`close.rate_changes.MESSAGE`) and is written with the code (supervisor ruling R-122 (b)). No status, problem or copy id is added; 04 rev 1.297 | §5.5 IMP-144 (with the code); revision header |
| 1.209 | 2026-10-03 | Lane SECFIX-PLT (item POLICY-OVERRIDE-WITHDRAW-1 — the one item placed behind the freeze of release 1.0; supervisor ruling R-126 (b) (4) and (c), on Ray's delegation of 2026-10-03, and the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-03 on the lane's line; docs first; number assigned by the supervisor, register index 308; 04 rev 1.322; row appended at the table's tail). A policy override for a contract or an obligation could be requested, approved by a second person and stored, and no computation read it; in release 1.0 the request is refused by name and the screens no longer make it (lane F-CTR-WEB's half, SCREENS rev 1.80). **§2.5** the routing row of `POLICY_OVERRIDE` and **§5.6 ACT-55** are marked not offered in release 1.0; the routing rule and the permission stay as the rule of the stored subject. **§5.5 ERR-102** — `policy-level-not-allowed` with `errors[].rule_id = "POLICY_OVERRIDE_NOT_OFFERED"`, 422, "Policy level not allowed": `POST /policy-overrides`, every creation — "Policy overrides for a contract or an obligation are not offered in this release." and what decides the parameter instead; nothing is stored. The row itself joins the table with the code that answers it, in the commit after this one: the copy catalogue's test holds a copy row, its rule id and the code's sentence together. ERR-47 keeps its copy for a registry value at a level its parameter does not list. No new status or problem type. | §2.5; §5.5 ERR-102; §5.6 ACT-55; revision header |
| 1.210 | 2026-10-03 | Lane SECFIX-CLO (item PRODUCT-POLICY-VALUE-NOT-READ-1 — the second item placed behind the freeze of release 1.0; supervisor ruling R-126 (c) and the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-03 on the lane's list and words; docs first; number assigned by the supervisor, register index 309; 04 rev 1.323; POLICIES rev 1.125; row appended at the table's tail). A product and an obligation template could state a value of three policy parameters that the books read for a contract and never from a product or a template: the value was stored, approved with the template's version and used by no computation. **§5.5 ERR-103** — `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "POLICY_PRODUCT_LEVEL_NOT_READ"` on `policy_values.<key>`, 422, "Check the highlighted fields": the three doors of a product's policy values and the two of an obligation template version refuse such a value and store nothing, with one of two sentences, each naming the parameter by its code — for `usage.tier_minimum_method` and `upfront_fee.recognition_period` that the framework's default applies to every contract, which may be left out or stated; for `pob.shipping_as_fulfilment` that the legal entity's or the workspace's value applies where the framework does not fix it, set by a policy version. IMP-46 and IMP-47 keep their copy for the two older findings of the check. No new status, problem type, permission or screen: the obligation template editor shows the sentence in its refusal banner, as it shows the check's other sentences (SCREENS §11.2 rev 1.81), and no screen edits a product's policy values. | §5.5 ERR-103; revision header |

## 0. How to use this document

### 0.1 Identifier families

| Family | Format | Meaning | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Persona | `PRS-nn`, sub-persona `PRS-nna` | A role-bearing user archetype (§1) | Stable; never renumbered |
| Segment | `S1`..`S4` | Company-size segments of `docs/03-REQUIREMENTS.md` §2.1 | Reused verbatim |
| Product position | `PP-nn` | A product stance derived from the personas (§1.5) | Stable |
| World object | `WLD-T-nn` tenant, `WLD-U-nn` user (`WLD-U-Rn` credential rule), `WLD-C-nn` customer, `WLD-K-nn` key contract, `WLD-X-nn` expected figure, `WLD-F-nn` fixture file, `WLD-B-nn` seeded open-period item, `WLD-R-nn` generation rule, `WLD-P-nn` period rule | Sample world used by the seed, the screens and the e2e tests (§2) | Seed data carries these ids in `external_id` or seed manifests so tests can find them |
| Surface | `SF-nn` | A navigable product surface or shell element (§3) | Stable. SCREENS (`docs/design/SCREENS.md` and `SCREENS_B.md`, D-74) specifies every surface and owns its route path (D-73); this document fixes no route path |
| Journey | `J-nn`; step `J-nn.s`; acceptance `J-nn-AC-n`; alternate path `J-nn-ALT-n` | End-to-end user journeys (§4) | One journey module per journey (§0.4) |
| State machine | `SM-nn` | Lifecycle of a product object (§5.2) | Each state is either a `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` §3 literal of the named enum, in the enum's exact case, or a behaviour mapped to a job, an open approval request or a child row's state per 04 §0.5 (SMAP rows). No PRD-only state exists; UI labels are copy (D-73) |
| Business rule | `BR-<AREA>-nn` | Product rule (§5.3). AREA is the register area code | Testable statement |
| Notification | `NTF-nn` | In-app and email notification (§5.4) | Kinds are `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` E-69 values |
| Error copy | `ERR-nn` (problem response copy), `IMP-nn` (finding and exception code copy) | User-facing error text (§5.5) | Slugs, statuses, codes and severities are 04 §15.2 and §15.4 verbatim (D-73). Wording is binding for the UI; the parity suite asserts codes, not wording |
| Action | `ACT-nn` | A permissioned user action (§5.6) | Permission codes are `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` T-PLT-11 codes |
| Non-functional requirement | `NFR-nn` | §6 | Measurable |
| Legacy transition | `LTM-nn` | Mapping of a legacy button or template to eRev Cloud (§7) | 14 buttons plus 4 templates |

### 0.2 Facts, assumptions and judgements

- **[F] Fact:** a statement about a source document, cited by section.
- **[A] Assumption:** a premise this document relies on that another document owns. Each is listed where used and, where material, in §9.
- **[J] Judgement:** a product call made here, with a one-line rationale.
- Requirement statements use "must" implicitly. Every journey step and business rule is observable through the UI or the API.

### 0.3 Relationship to other documents

| Question | Where it is answered |
|---|---|
| Is a capability in 1.0, and at what priority? | `docs/03-REQUIREMENTS.md` (this document cites REQ ids and never changes release or priority) |
| What number must the engine produce? | Answer keys and `docs/accounting/POLICIES.md`. The expected figures in §2.8 were computed from the stated inputs under D-11 as amended by D-11a (ROUND_HALF_UP, largest remainder, and cumulative rounding of the exact cumulative amount: C_t = round_half_up(X × f_t), bounded by the posted allocation A and equal to A at completion; POLICIES ALG-01 §2.1.3) and POL-090 `DAILY`; where a figure reuses a research 04 answer key, the key id is cited. If an answer key and this document ever disagree, the answer key wins and this document is corrected (D-71). |
| Which table or endpoint implements a behaviour? | `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md`, `docs/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` |
| Which literal, permission code, problem slug or finding code? | `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` §3, T-PLT-11, §15.2 and §15.4 (D-73); registry keys and option literals: POLICIES §1 (D-13a). This document cites them verbatim |
| What does a screen look like, and what is its route? | `docs/design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`; SCREENS (`docs/design/SCREENS.md` and `SCREENS_B.md`, D-74), which owns the route path of each SF id (D-73) |

### 0.4 How journeys become tests

| Rule | Content |
|---|---|
| E2E-01 | Each journey J-nn has one journey module `frontend/e2e/journeys/j-nn-<slug>.journey.ts`, executed by the project specs under `frontend/e2e/projects/` (`docs/dev-guide.md` §0.5 and DG-E2E-03 own the path; rev 1.1). Each `J-nn-AC-n` is one named step, and its title starts with the AC id. |
| E2E-02 | Money is asserted twice: the API value as an exact decimal string (for example `"91463.41"`) and the rendered text per DS-FMT-04/05/06 (for example `91,463.41`). Floats are never used in assertions (DS-FMT-02). |
| E2E-03 | The e2e database `erev_e2e` is reseeded once per `make e2e` run by the deterministic demo generator (REQ-DEMO-007). |
| E2E-04 | **Serial project `avenmoor-serial`**, one worker, fixed order: J-02, J-03, J-04, J-05, J-06, J-07, J-08, J-09, J-10, J-11, J-12, J-16, J-19, J-26, J-13, J-14, J-15, J-17, J-18, J-25, J-22. These journeys share WLD-T-01 and later journeys depend on earlier outcomes (for example J-14 reopens the period J-13 locks). |
| E2E-05 | **Fresh-tenant project** (parallel workers): J-01, J-20, J-21, J-23. Each creates its own tenant through the operator command of BR-PLT-01 (REQ-PLT-038; 04 §14.3) in its `beforeAll` and never touches WLD-T-01. |
| E2E-06 | **Industry project**: J-24 on WLD-T-02. Read-only. |
| E2E-07 | Wall-clock independence: journeys pass explicit effective dates and periods. The UI passes `as_of` = the end date of the context period (BR-UX-02), so no acceptance depends on the date the tests run. `recorded_at` values are asserted only for order and presence. |
| E2E-08 | Demo credentials and TOTP secrets come from environment variables (§2.3). Specs read them from the environment and never embed them. |
| E2E-09 | Every journey ends with an axe scan of each surface it visited (G8, NFR-20). |

## 1. Personas and jobs to be done (M-PM-03)

`docs/03-REQUIREMENTS.md` §2 defines the segments S1-S4 and the default roles [F]. This section adds the named personas, their jobs, how the jobs change by company size, and the product positions that follow.

### 1.1 Segments and typical tenant setup

| Segment [F: REQ §2.1] | Typical eRev Cloud setup [J] | Personas present | Sample-world tenant |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 Small company: below about USD 20M revenue, QuickBooks Online or Xero, Stripe, one or two accountants, no SOX | One entity; `ASC606` book; `billing.posting = ERP` (POL-004; D-13a); Stripe inbound and QuickBooks Online outbound (mocks) or CSV; one approval step; a published auto-approval rule for Stripe-originated standard subscriptions (REQ-PLT-016); MFA only for approvers (REQ-PLT-005) | PRS-01 (also SSP analyst); PRS-02 played by the controller or an outside CPA; PRS-04; PRS-05 as investor due-diligence reviewer or first-year auditor; PRS-06 played by an IT contractor | WLD-T-20 (J-01) |
| S2 Mid-market: USD 20M-250M, 2-10 entities, NetSuite or Intacct, Salesforce, pre-IPO | 2-10 entities and currencies; `IFRS15` book for foreign statutory reporting; Salesforce inbound and NetSuite outbound (mocks); threshold routing; monthly close with reconciliations | PRS-01 (2-6 people), PRS-02, PRS-03, PRS-04, PRS-05, PRS-06, PRS-06b, PRS-07 | WLD-T-01, WLD-T-22 |
| S3 Enterprise and public, SOX 404(b) | Many entities; entity-scoped roles (REQ-PLT-012); multi-level routing; access review campaigns (REQ-CTL-006); an evidence pack at every lock (REQ-RPT-014) | All personas, including PRS-03a, PRS-05a and PRS-06a | WLD-T-01; the volume tenant `perf-volume` of REQ-OPS-009 for performance (D-75 Q7) |
| S4 Legacy eRev user | Legacy-parity preset (REQ-POL-005); legacy v1 templates (REQ-DAT-002); `LEGACY` book with `je.posting_mode = DELTA` for adjustment journals (POL-005; REQ-BK-004); `ASC606.db` migration (REQ-MIG-001 to 004) | PRS-01 (the former desktop operator), PRS-02, PRS-03 | WLD-T-00, WLD-T-20, WLD-T-21, WLD-T-23 |

### 1.3 Jobs to be done

Each job is written "When…, I want…, so that…". The journey column shows where the job is proven.

| Persona | # | Job | Journey |
|---|---|---|---|
| PRS-01 | 1 | When a deal closes in the CRM, I want the contract to arrive with obligations, SSPs and allocation already proposed, so that I review only the judgement points instead of re-keying lines. | J-03, J-19 |
| PRS-01 | 2 | When delivery, usage, cost or billing data lands, I want one validated import with a dry-run diff, so that errors surface before anything posts. | J-04, J-10, J-11, J-12 |
| PRS-01 | 3 | When a customer changes a contract, I want the system to classify the modification and show its revenue effect before I submit, so that my memo and the numbers agree. | J-05, J-06 |
| PRS-01 | 4 | When an estimate moves (VC, EAC, return rate, redemption), I want to record a new version with evidence and see the catch-up, so that the change is traceable to the version pair (D-20). | J-07, J-10 |
| PRS-01 | 5 | When a number is questioned, I want its derivation and source row one click away, so that I answer without rebuilding a spreadsheet. | J-16 |
| PRS-02 | 1 | When items await my approval, I want one inbox with the diff, the impact preview and the evidence, so that my decision is recorded against exactly what I saw (REQ-PLT-014, 015). | J-02 to J-12 |
| PRS-02 | 2 | When a preparer departs from the proposed treatment, I want the rationale and judgement record in front of me, so that overrides are deliberate. | J-05-ALT-1 |
| PRS-02 | 3 | When I review an SSP change, I want the historical evidence and the version diff, so that allocations rest on a defensible basis. | J-02 |
| PRS-03 | 1 | When month-end arrives, I want a cockpit of blockers, gates and sign-offs per entity and book, so that I lock only when the evidence is complete. | J-13 |
| PRS-03 | 2 | When an error is found after lock, I want a controlled reopen with two approvers and a diff at re-lock, so that corrections are visible to auditors. | J-14 |
| PRS-03 | 3 | When a policy or configuration changes, I want simulation and prospective effect, so that prior periods are never restated silently. | J-01 (preset), BR-POL-01 |
| PRS-03 | 4 | When the auditor asks, I want the evidence pack generated at lock, so that PBC requests take minutes. | J-15, J-17 |
| PRS-04 | 1 | When I open the product, I want revenue, contract liability, RPO and close status for the period with drill-down, so that I brief the board from system figures. | J-24 |
| PRS-04 | 2 | When a figure surprises me, I want Explain, so that I can trust or challenge it without asking a preparer. | J-16 |
| PRS-05 | 1 | When fieldwork starts, I want the period evidence pack with a manifest of hashes, so that I can rely on report completeness and accuracy (research 07 A-18). | J-17 |
| PRS-05 | 2 | When I select samples, I want a contract sample pack from executed terms to journal line (A-04, A-20). | J-17 |
| PRS-05 | 3 | When I test journal entries, I want the complete population with preparer, approver, UTC timestamps and post-close flags, tied to journal totals (A-01). | J-17 |
| PRS-05 | 4 | When I test IT general controls, I want the user listing, SoD report, configuration change register and audit-chain verification (A-15, A-16, A-19). | J-17 |
| PRS-06 | 1 | When someone joins or moves, I want to grant scoped roles with SoD checks and approval, so that access is right the first time (CU-01). | J-22 |
| PRS-06 | 2 | When enabling AI, I want explicit settings and call logs, so that data handling is provable (D-46). | J-19 (precondition) |
| PRS-06b | 1 | When connecting CRM, billing and ERP, I want test connections, control totals and an exception queue, so that interface errors never reach the ledger silently (CU-05). | J-23 |
| PRS-07 | 1 | When a rep proposes a non-standard deal, I want allocation and revenue timing from approved SSPs before signature, so that pricing includes the accounting effect. | J-18 |
| PRS-07 | 2 | When planning, I want forecast event sets run by the production engine in a scenario tenant, so that forecasts use the same rules as actuals (README acct #10). | J-18 |
| PRS-08 | 1 | When a source object changes, I want to send it with an idempotency key, so that retries never double count (REQ-PLT-026). | J-11, J-23 |
| PRS-08 | 2 | When the ERP acknowledges a journal batch, I want the receipt recorded, so that lock gates can verify posting (REQ-JE-016). | J-13 |

Pains carried from legacy eRev [F]: uploads applied immediately with no preview or approval (legacy 02 §1.4); silent row drops and swallowed errors (`docs/legacy/DEVIATIONS.md` §5); no period locks ("eRev doesn't freeze periods", REQ-CLS-001 legacy column); history protected only by copying the database file (legacy 01 §1.5).

### 1.4 How each job changes by segment

| Persona | S1 Small company | S2 Mid-market | S3 Enterprise and public | S4 Legacy eRev user |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PRS-01 | One person prepares everything, including SSP analysis; data from Stripe and CSV | Several preparers split by entity; Salesforce and billing feeds; usage and modifications are frequent | Preparers scoped to entities; volume imports; exceptions triaged daily | Keeps the four templates; learns the dry-run diff and approval step |
| PRS-02 | Played by the controller or an outside CPA who logs in monthly; bulk approval matters (REQ-PLT-017) | A revenue manager approves daily with threshold routing | Multi-level routing; delegation during absence (REQ-PLT-013) | Approves template imports that legacy applied immediately |
| PRS-03 | Also the reviewer for configuration; closes one entity | Closes several entities with one multi-entity command (REQ-ENT-006); signs reconciliations | Certifies close under SOX; two approvers other than the requester approve reopens, at least one a Controller (REQ-CLS-011); access review campaigns | Receives period locks for the first time |
| PRS-04 | Founder or fractional CFO; needs Home and RPO for investors | Board pack and forecast comparison | Disclosure pack review before filing | Replaces the legacy Excel exports with reports |
| PRS-05 | Investor or first-year auditor wants a rollforward and a JE population | Statutory and US GAAP audits; IFRS 15 book | SOC 1 reliance, IPE testing, JE fraud testing (AS 2401) | Asks how migrated history ties to the legacy database (CTL-048) |
| PRS-06 | Part-time IT contractor; few users | IT admin plus a separate integration admin | Separate security officer approves access; SoD exceptions with compensating controls | First multi-user setup; replaces sharing one SQLite file (README ops #3) |
| PRS-07 | Rare; the founder previews deals | Deal desk previews non-standard deals weekly | FP&A runs quarterly forecast sets | Replaces "reset the database and re-run" (README ops #4c) |
| PRS-08 | Stripe mock client | Salesforce, NetSuite and metering clients | Many clients with rotation and inventory reviews (CU-12) | None at first |

### 1.5 Product positions

| Id | Position [J] | Rationale | REQ |
|---|---|---|---|
| PP-01 | Every approval needs a second human. There is no single-user mode and no self-approval waiver. S1 companies name an outside accountant as reviewer; system-originated standard items may auto-approve only under a published auto-approval rule. | Maker-checker is the control auditors rely on; a bypass makes it untestable. | REQ-PLT-011, 013, 016 |
| PP-02 | Viewers (including the CFO persona) do not approve by default; approval rights come only from approval roles. | Keeps SoD analysis on roles, not titles. | REQ-PLT-008, 010 |
| PP-03 | Auditors self-serve from lock snapshots. Their reads and exports are audited. They never see credentials or secrets. | Reduces PBC cycle time without weakening access control (research 07 §7). | REQ-PLT-008, REQ-RPT-014 |
| PP-04 | Automation never approves: service accounts and AI hold no approval permission; published auto-approval rules are the only non-human approvals and name their rule id and version. | D-46; REQ-PLT-011. | REQ-PLT-016, REQ-AI-003 |
| PP-05 | Legacy users start under the legacy-parity preset and move to native policies through a prospective policy change. | Proves parity first, then improves accounting without restating history. | REQ-MIG-005, REQ-POL-007 |
| PP-06 | Forecasts and deal previews never write to production. | Scenario outputs are not books (GLOSSARY "Book"). | REQ-FC-001, 006; REQ-PLT-022 |
| PP-07 | Every computed figure opens Explain, and the UI never shows a signed position. | D-12, D-60. | REQ-RPT-018, REQ-BIL-003 |
| PP-08 | Nothing fails silently: a rejected input is always a finding in the dry-run diff or an exception item. Findings are blocking errors where the input cannot be processed correctly and warnings where it can. | D-30, D-30a. | REQ-DAT-005, 009 |

## 2. Sample world

The sample world is one data set used three ways: the seed (`make seed`, REQ-DEMO-001 to 007), the screens (every navigable surface renders it, REQ-DEMO-006) and the e2e journeys (§4). Its expected figures (§2.8) are the numeric acceptance of the journeys.

### 2.1 Generation rules

| Id | Rule | Rationale [J] |
|---|---|---|
| WLD-R-01 | One deterministic generator (REQ-DEMO-007) with the fixed seed `20260912`. The same inputs produce byte-identical expected-output files. | Reproducible demos and tests |
| WLD-R-02 | The seed builds history through the product's commands, executed as the persona users: imports are uploaded by one user and approved by another, periods are closed through close runs, and locks are certified. Direct table inserts are allowed only for global catalogues (currencies, permissions, engine release). | Seeded records then carry audit events, approvals and run records, so registers and evidence packs are populated |
| WLD-R-03 | Tenant and customer display names are fictitious and end with "(Demo)" (REQ-DEMO-004). They are placeholders that need trademark screening before any public release (research 05 §26; §9 Q6). | Clean room |
| WLD-R-04 | The seed refuses to write to a tenant whose `is_demo` is false, and refuses to create or change users outside the `demo.erev` email domain (REQ-DEMO-005). | Protects real tenants |
| WLD-R-05 | Integrations point only at the in-process mock adapters mounted under `/api/v1/__mocks__/<adapter>` when `EREV_ENV` ∈ {`dev`, `test`, `e2e`} (D-72; REQ-INT-003). No mock process binds a port, and there is no outbound network (REQ-SEC-008). | G§5 |
| WLD-R-06 | Background contracts: Avenmoor 180 (AVM-US 100, AVM-UK 30, AVM-DE 40, AVM-JP 10); each industry tenant as listed in §2.10. Background external ids follow `BG-<tenant code>-<nnnn>`. Tests never assert background values directly; they assert consistency only (a dashboard figure equals its report total). | Lists and dashboards look real without making tests brittle |
| WLD-R-07 | Tests locate records by external id (source system + source id, REQ-DAT-011) or by name, never by generated sequence numbers (`CON-`, `MOD-`, `APR-`). | Sequence numbers depend on seed order |
| WLD-R-08 | Seeding every demo tenant completes in ≤ 15 minutes locally (NFR-08). | Keeps `make e2e` practical |

### 2.2 Demo clock and period states

| Id | Rule |
|---|---|
| WLD-P-01 | Avenmoor and the six industry tenants go live on 2026-01-01. No key contract has an inception date before go-live. [J] Bounds seed cost; opening balances are exercised by J-20. |
| WLD-P-02 | For every entity and book, the E-04 `period_state` is: Jan to Aug 2026 `closed` (each locked through a close run by `marcus@demo.erev`); Sep 2026 `open`, the current period; Oct 2026 to Dec 2027 `future`. Rev 1.153 (item DEMO-CLOCK-1; 05 SCH-05 rev 1.163): the clock opens no period of a demo tenant, so the world keeps this month whatever the day a stack runs; a `future` period opens by hand (`POST /periods/{id}/open`) or by the lock of the period before it (BR-CLS-03). Rev 1.162 (demo world, item 12; BUILD_SPEC CLO-22; dev-guide DG-MK-seed): the closed months are a stage that the seed runs when it is asked — `erev seed demo --with-close`, `make seed CLOSE=1` — because a lock through the commands took 142 to 184 seconds inside a seed (three locks measured on 2026-10-01, four fifths of each in the three period-end passes of the close run; item PERF-CLOSE-FANOUT-1) and the seed runs before every end-to-end stack; a seed that is not asked leaves Jan to Sep 2026 `open` in every entity and book. The stage closes AVM-US in `ASC606`, Jan to Aug 2026, each month as the personas (WLD-R-02): a close run that `maya` starts; its journal run, which `maya` submits, `priya` approves and `maya` exports and acknowledges with the ledger's document reference — a CSV batch, the world holding no ledger connection in those months (BR-JE-03); the two reconciliations the lock requires, prepared by `maya` and reviewed by `priya`; and the lock, which `marcus` decides on `maya`'s request. The trial balance of a seeded month is written from the subledger's closing balances: the subledger-to-GL reconciliation shows the tie-out's form, not two independent books. The billing-to-subledger reconciliation lists the billing events the seed records by hand as unmatched, and `maya` explains each, until the world holds billing documents. The open items of §2.9 that sit on AVM-US hold every lock made after them, so the stage makes them after its locks (WLD-P-04). The other entity-books of Avenmoor and the six industry tenants are not closed at this revision; AVM-JP cannot be closed as it is seeded: the engine finds no SSP for three of its background licences, and a close run of the entity ends `BLOCKED` on them. |
| WLD-P-03 | Mandatory lock-and-reopen history (REQ-DEMO-003): in each tenant the first entity's Jun 2026 was locked, reopened with two approvals (`marcus`, `elena`) for a documented correction, and re-locked, producing a re-lock diff report. Rev 1.162: not seeded yet. The stage of WLD-P-02 locks each of its months once; the reopen and the re-lock of Jun 2026 belong between June's lock and July's, because a period reopens only while no later period is closed (BR-CLS-05). |
| WLD-P-04 | Lock, approval and event `recorded_at` values are server timestamps at seed time (D-19: never client-supplied). The seed executes periods in order, so `recorded_at` order matches effective order except for the deliberate late items in §2.9. |
| WLD-P-05 | AVM-JP uses a monthly calendar with an April fiscal-year start. Period labels remain Gregorian (`Sep 2026`, DS-FMT-19); fiscal quarters display as `FY2027 Q2`. The fiscal year is named by the calendar year in which it ends. The API `period_key` follows 04 API-C-11 (for example `FY2027-P06` for Sep 2026). [J] Matches common Japanese practice and exercises REQ-REF-002 configuration. |
| WLD-P-06 | The context pill defaults to the earliest `open` period of the context entity in the primary book (BR-UX-01), which is Sep 2026 in the seeded tenants. rc note (D-90a QA-L9-1): without seeded close history (R-RC-1; CLO-22, DMO-4) the earliest open period of the seeded entities is FY2026-P01 (Jan 2026), which BR-UX-01 and SCR-URL-20 select (`docs/reviews/loop/sprint/L5-4.md:54`). Rev 1.162: a world seeded with its close (WLD-P-02) opens AVM-US in `ASC606` on Sep 2026; its other entity-books, and every world seeded without the close — the end-to-end stack's among them — open on FY2026-P01 as the rc note says. |

Published approval routing (`APPROVAL_ROUTING` version 1) and auto-approval (`AUTO_APPROVAL` version 1) rule sets:

| Subject (E-08) | Condition | Steps (each approver differs from the preparer and from earlier approvers) |
|---|---|---|
| `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` | Manually created, AI-assisted, or TP ≥ USD 100,000.00 functional equivalent, or any non-standard flag, or the activation of a `NOT_A_CONTRACT` contract (the criteria-met activation of SM-02, whatever its origin, amount and flags; rev 1.55, supervisor ruling R-61 (c)), or an activation while a book of the contracting entity is assessed not probable (rev 1.67, supervisor ruling R-77 (2)), or the criteria-met request of an active contract of which a book is not a contract (rev 1.79, supervisor ruling R-102 (a)) | 1: `contract.approve` (Revenue Reviewer). If TP ≥ USD 1,000,000.00, or if the amount cannot be stated for want of a spot rate (rev 1.194): 2: `contract.approve` held by a Controller |
| `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` | Originated by `svc-salesforce`, TP < USD 100,000.00, no flags, the contract is `DRAFT` and no book is assessed not probable: a criteria-met activation, and an activation that leaves a book not a contract, are not standard items and are never auto-approved (REQ-PLT-016; rev 1.55, rev 1.67) | Auto-approve under rule `AUTO-CON-01` |
| `MODIFICATION` | Any | 1: `modification.approve` (Revenue Reviewer). If absolute catch-up ≥ USD 50,000.00 or absolute TP change ≥ USD 250,000.00: 2: `modification.approve` held by a Controller |
| `ESTIMATE_VERSION` | Any | 1: `estimate.approve` (Revenue Reviewer). If absolute P&L impact ≥ USD 50,000.00: 2: Controller |
| `MANUAL_EVENT`, `JUDGEMENT_RECORD`, `COMBINATION_GROUP`, `SSP_OVERRIDE` | Any | 1: `event.approve`, `judgement.review`, `contract.approve` or `ssp.approve` respectively |
| `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` | Absolute amount < USD 10,000.00 | 1: `adjustment.approve` |
| `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` | Absolute amount ≥ USD 10,000.00 (attachment required: a document whose file can still be read, rev 1.145) | 1: `adjustment.approve` (Revenue Reviewer); 2: `adjustment.approve` held by a Controller |
| `IMPORT_COMMIT` | Uploaded by a user | 1: `import.approve` |
| `IMPORT_COMMIT` | Uploaded by an API client, control totals match, zero `ERROR` findings | Auto-approve under rule `AUTO-IMP-01` |
| `IMPORT_COMMIT` | Any upload of a template whose commit performs an approval by itself: legacy v1 SKU SSP (approves the SSP book version), Contract Setup (activates the contract, reviews its judgement records, approves its variable consideration estimates) and Contract Modification (approves the modification) | Never auto-approved. 1: `import.approve`, by an approver who also holds — in their own right — the underlying subject's permission for every legal entity the import names (rev 1.36); 2: the underlying subject's second step — its permission and role — when the file's content triggers it, and any step a routing rule of the workspace adds to the underlying subject (rev 1.33 part 7; supervisor rulings R-92, R-98) — a rule on the underlying subject's functional amount too: each approval the commit performs is routed with the amount its own request would carry (rev 1.127; ruling R-109 (d)). The import takes exactly the approvers the direct path would: one person with both permissions approves an unflagged import; a flagged one takes a second person, who answers for every underlying second step. Each approver is checked at their own decision, approve and reject alike |
| `EVIDENCE_SHRED` | A file that a record holds as its evidence and an approval may release (the source of a committed import, the source file of a signed reconciliation, the legacy database of a migration, an SSP study, the attachment of a manual adjustment and, since rev 1.185, the evidence of recorded events); requested by a holder of `settings.manage` (step-up) with the reason of the erasure request (rev 1.71); the requester's permission covers every legal entity of the records that reference the file, as the permission of an administrator who shreds a file directly does (rev 1.156); the request names the approval requests that are pending on the records that hold the file — the decision of each is refused once the file is shredded (rev 1.145) | Never auto-approved. 1: `config.approve` held by a Controller whose scope covers every legal entity of the records that hold the file, or every entity when one of them has none; the requester cannot approve |
| `SSP_BOOK_VERSION` | Any | 1: `ssp.approve`. If any entry changes its method or its mid value by more than 10%: 2: `ssp.approve` (REQ-SSP-007) |
| `REGISTRY_VERSION`, `RULE_SET_VERSION`, `POB_TEMPLATE_VERSION`, `ACCOUNT_MAPPING_VERSION`, `FX_RATE_SET_VERSION`, `PRINCIPAL_AGENT_CHANGE`, `MAPPING_PROFILE_VERSION` | Any | 1: `config.approve` (Controller) |
| `POLICY_OVERRIDE` | Any contract- or obligation-level policy value (T-CON-23; POLICIES §0.6 OVR). Not offered in release 1.0 (rev 1.209; ERR-102): no override can be created, so no request of this subject is made; the row stays the rule of the stored subject | 1: `contract.approve` (Revenue Reviewer). When the parameter's approval code is `JDG`, the linked judgement record is reviewed in the same session (rev 1.1; §9 Q11) |
| `ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE` | Change of accounts, entity or SSP version pin of an existing obligation (event `LINE_ATTRIBUTES_CHANGED`, REQ-MOD-021) | 1: `event.approve` (Revenue Reviewer) (rev 1.1; §9 Q11) |
| `AI_PROPOSAL_ACCEPTANCE` | Accepted proposal fields applied to an existing record under approval (SM-11) | 1: `contract.approve` (Revenue Reviewer) (rev 1.1; §9 Q11) |
| `EXCEPTION_WAIVER` | Any (T-IMP-05 `waiver_approval_request_id`) | 1: `exception.waive`, held by a user other than the requester and the item owner (rev 1.1; §9 Q11) |
| `JOURNAL_RUN` | Any | 1: `journal.approve` |
| `PERIOD_LOCK` | Lock or permanent lock (E-63 `LOCK`, `PERMANENT_LOCK`) | 1: `period.lock` (Controller) |
| `PERIOD_REOPEN` | Any | 1 step with `min_approvers = 2`: `period.reopen_approve` (Controller or Revenue Reviewer, §5.6); at least one approver holds Controller; neither approver is the requester (REQ-CLS-011; D-75 Q12) |
| `MIGRATION_PROMOTION` | Any | 1: `migration.approve` (Controller), by a user other than the runner; unexplained differences 0 (BR-MIG-02) (rev 1.1; B1-026) |
| `MIGRATION_SSP_REPLAY` | Submitted at `/import` by a user; the legacy `SKU_SSP` rows carry zero LM-SSP-01..09 `ERROR` findings; content bound to the source digest | Auto-approve under rule `AUTO-MIG-01`; the human control point stays `MIGRATION_PROMOTION`, whose content names the replayed `LEGACY-SKU-SSP` versions (rev 1.15; D-98 133 AMENDMENT 4; 04 1.72) |
| `CONTRACT_VOID` | No posted line exists | 1: `contract.approve` (Revenue Reviewer) |
| `CONTRACT_VOID` | Any posted line exists | 1: `contract.approve` (Revenue Reviewer); 2: `contract.approve` held by a Controller (the published rule's second step carries the `controller` role reference, 04 T-REF-26 `role`, D-98 93; a posted-state change between submission and decision voids the pending request as stale and the void is resubmitted under the route its posted state now requires, D-98 92) |
| `ROLE_ASSIGNMENT`, `ROLE_CHANGE`, `SOD_EXCEPTION`, `SUPPORT_GRANT` | Any | 1: `access.approve` or `support_grant.approve` (Tenant Admin other than the requester). Rev 1.97: the scopes of a new API client are an access grant and are approved as a `ROLE_ASSIGNMENT` (ACT-46; REQ-PLT-033) |
| `IMPORT_COMMIT`, `MANUAL_EVENT`, `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT` in a `reopened` period | Any | Rule above plus BR-CLS-06 (SoD-6) |

Rev 1.33 (supervisor ruling R-26; 04 §16.10 "Auto-approval and the routing floor"). The rows above are the published rules of the sample world, and two limits hold for every tenant's rules. A routing rule adds steps to the approval a subject has without any rule, or raises them; it never replaces the subject's permission, lowers its approver count, or drops its second step, its Controller or the `PERIOD_REOPEN` quorum. The three "Auto-approve" rows are the items a rule may approve, each for the originator the row names (an integration's contract, an API client's import, the legacy SSP replay a user starts at import), together with the setup grants of BR-PLT-02; a rule approves nothing else — no configuration version, no rule set, no other access change, no period lock or reopen, no journal run, and no item a user prepares. Supervisor rulings R-38 and R-41 refine the two limits. *System-originated means the content, not only the submitter:* the `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` row "Originated by `svc-salesforce`" applies only when every event of the contract's stream was written by an integration — a draft a person replaced, edited or recorded an event on, or changed through an approval of its own (an override, an estimate version, an attribute change, a combination), is "manually created" in the sense of the first row, and a system job acting on behalf of a user is not an integration; an `IMPORT_COMMIT` never stands in for a stricter approval of what its commit puts in force (an activated contract, an SSP version, an estimate version, an account mapping, an FX rate set); and the legacy SSP replay and the setup grants are approved only by the rule sets provisioning seeds for them (`AUTO-MIG-01`, `AUTO-BOOTSTRAP`), never by a tenant's own rule. *The floor:* the Controller of the `PERIOD_LOCK` row and the Controller's second step of the `ESTIMATE_VERSION` row belong to the approval those subjects have without any rule, like the second steps of the other rows; approvers are counted per permission and role, so a rule that already asks for the Controller's step — in whatever order — is not given a second one. The P&L impact of an estimate version is the catch-up of its dry run at submission; it is compared with USD 50,000.00 at the spot rate to USD on the version's effective date, and a version whose impact cannot be measured because no such rate is in force takes the second step (04 T-PLT-17 `flags`, `PL_IMPACT_GE_50K`); the impact sums the catch-up of every contract of the combination group, in the tenant's primary book (supervisor ruling R-66 (4)). Rev 1.194 (item ACT-FLAGS-1; the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-02; 04 T-PLT-17, §16.1 and §16.10 rev 1.287; supervisor ruling R-66 (4)). *What "any non-standard flag" is.* An activation carries a flag, and waits for a person, for each of these facts of its contract: termination for convenience, a customer acceptance clause, consideration payable to the customer, noncash consideration, a line outside the scope of the standard, a line that states an SSP justification or its own accounts, a significant financing component (`NON_STANDARD_TERMS`); a side letter (`SIDE_LETTER`); an estimated element of variable consideration, returns, a price concession, breakage or royalties (`VARIABLE_CONSIDERATION`); a customer option that is a material right (`MATERIAL_RIGHT`); a product no activated contract of the workspace holds yet (`NEW_SKU`) — every product's first activation carries it, so at go-live the first contract of every product waits for a person; and a booking that does not state whether the contract holds an acceptance clause and whether a side letter exists (`TERMS_NOT_STATED`): what the record cannot tell is not presumed. An integration states the two terms on its order (05 ADP-16, ADP-17); the booking form and the import templates do not state them yet, so a contract a person books or imports carries the flag. *The two thresholds are US dollar amounts.* "TP ≥ USD 100,000.00" and "TP ≥ USD 1,000,000.00" read the larger of the booked consideration and the transaction price of the activation's dry run, converted to US dollars; the request states the same amount in the entity's functional currency. Every US dollar threshold of this table is converted in one way: at the engine's spot rate — the latest rate on or before the day — for the contract's inception date of an activation and for the effective date of a modification, of an estimate version and of a manual adjustment. For a member of a combined group the price is the group's, so each member of a group at or above USD 1,000,000.00 takes the Controller's step at its own activation, the second member as the first: each activation is its own decision. An amount that cannot be judged is treated as above, never as below: without the rate to the US dollar the activation carries `RATE_NOT_PUBLISHED` with both threshold flags, and an activation that cannot state its amount in the entity's functional currency carries `RATE_NOT_PUBLISHED` too; that flag takes the Controller's step by itself. A workspace that publishes no spot rate from a contract's currency to the US dollar therefore sends every activation of such a contract to a Revenue Reviewer and a Controller until it publishes one.

The mapping version `AVM-MAP-2026-01` is effective 2026-01-01 and was approved by `marcus`. Counter-entries use the D-14a roles (04 E-01; POLICIES §0.8): `COST_OF_REVENUE` for return-asset recognition and remeasurement (JET-07c), `CONTRACT_COST_CLEARING` for capitalised commissions (JET-09a), and `BILLING_CLEARING` with a `clearing_purpose` where another subledger owns the other side (JET-01b `UNAPPLIED_CASH`, JET-02 `AP_SUPPLIER`, JET-07d `INVENTORY`, JET-14 `EQUITY`, JET-17 `INVESTMENTS`; table 0.8-A). Roles without a mapping rule in Avenmoor: `PRE_STANDARD_REVENUE`, because no entity keeps a `LEGACY` book; `RECEIVABLE_CONTRA`, because `billing.posting = ERP` and JET-04c posts it only under `ENGINE`; and the reserved roles `RETAINED_EARNINGS` and `FINANCING_OBLIGATION`, which accept no mapping rule (04 E-01 rule 2). Journeys assert labelled balances; answer keys assert the counter-entries (rev 1.1; D-14a, B1-015; §9 Q9).

## 3. Product surfaces

The navigation is fixed by D-02 and REQ-UX-001: Home, Contracts, Schedules, Close, Journals, Reports, Policies, Data, Approvals, Settings. D-02 and REQ-UX-001 fix which ten destinations exist; DS-CMP-02 and SCREENS SCR-IA-01 fix the rail order (Home, Contracts, Schedules, Close, Journals, Reports, Approvals, Policies, Data, Settings; D-89 L7-3-Q-28). Obligations appear inside a contract. SF ids are stable. SCREENS (`docs/design/SCREENS.md` and `SCREENS_B.md`, D-74) owns the route path of every navigable surface (D-73), so this table fixes no path: navigable surfaces read "route per SCREENS", and shell elements state their placement. Context route and query parameters carry the 04 API-C-11 values verbatim: `entity` (entity code), `book` (`ASC606`, `IFRS15`, `LEGACY`) and `period` (the `period_key`, for example `FY2026-P09`). The context pill shows the DS-FMT-19 label (`Sep 2026`) (rev 1.1; B1-032; DS-CMP-03).

Shell rules:

| Id | Rule |
|---|---|
| BR-UX-01 | The context pill (entity, period, book) defaults to the user's last choice; if none, to the first entity in the user's scope in code order, its earliest `open` period (E-04) and the primary book. The pill's `entity`, `book` and `period` values are the API-C-11 identifiers (`period_key`). |
| BR-UX-02 | Every screen read passes `as_of` = the end date of the context period and `known_at` = now, unless the user sets "Known at" in the advanced read options or opens an "as locked" view (lock snapshot id). The API default of REQ-PLT-030 still applies to API callers. |
| BR-UX-03 | A sandbox tenant shows a persistent banner "Sandbox: <tenant name>. Nothing here posts or exports." on every screen (REQ-PLT-003). |
| BR-UX-04 | A user whose roles hold no command permission sees a "Read-only access" chip in the top bar. |
| BR-UX-05 | Screens show only actions the user may perform. A hidden action is also refused by the API (ACT table §5.6); the UI never relies on hiding for security. |
| BR-UX-06 | An object outside the user's entity scope is "not found" in the UI and 404 in the API (REQ-PLT-012). Rev 1.137 (item APR-CONTENT-SCOPE-1; 04 §16.10 rev 1.208): an approval request can name several legal entities. It is listed for a user who covers one of them, with its number, its status, its steps and who decided them, the user's own entities and the count of all. What it would put in force — the summary that names the record, the amount, the impact preview, the attachments and the approvers' comments — is shown only to a user who covers every entity it names, whoever submitted it; to anyone else the request says that this part is withheld (SCREENS §15.4), and its files are "not found". Rev 1.171 (item APR-REJECTION-REASON-PREPARER-1; 04 §16.10 rev 1.240): the reason of a rejection is told to the user who submitted the request — in the notification of the rejection and on the request itself — also where the rest of it is withheld from that user: the approver writes it to them, and they cannot correct what they are not told. Nothing else of the content is shown with it, and no other user who sees part of the request reads it. Rev 1.176 (item APR-REQUEST-REASON-1; 04 §16.10 rev 1.252): the reason and the comment a request was submitted with are part of what it would put in force, shown under the same rule — and to the user who submitted it, who reads their own words on the request also where the rest of it is withheld. What an approver writes with a decision is a comment and, through the API, a reason code: the two are shown together and withheld together (item APR-DECISION-CODE-CONTENT-1). Rev 1.148: what no entity owns is for a member whose permission covers ALL entities — the audit log and who acted in it, the control evidence, the tenant's settings and currencies, a snapshot, a sandbox or its reset, a webhook endpoint, an operator's support grant and its approval. A member whose role names entities is refused there (403), sees the jobs he started and no other, reads a source record through a contract in his scope, and runs a report over the entities inside every permission the report needs; the verifications of the audit chain he reads, because they state no entity's data (04 API-C-03). Rev 1.188: the workspace's own reference data is among what no entity owns. A new legal entity, a book and a fiscal calendar with its years are created and changed by a member whose permission covers all entities (ACT-44); a member whose role names entities changes those entities and the books they keep, is refused elsewhere (403) with the sentence that an administrator of all entities makes the change, and keeps a book the workspace has not enabled only after such an administrator has enabled it. The periods of a fiscal year are generated for every entity on the calendar (04 API-C-03). Rev 1.203 (item POLICY-TENANT-SCOPE-ALL-ENTITIES-1; 04 API-R-13 and §16.10 rev 1.309): a policy version of the tenant, and one of a book, is among what no entity owns, whatever its category — an accounting policy, a practical expedient, a disclosure election or a settings category. The tenant's version answers for every entity that states no value of its own, and a book's for every entity that keeps the book, ahead of the entity's own value (POLICIES §0.5 rule 2: a book's value is read before an entity's), so such a version is authored — created, changed, tested, submitted, withdrawn, published — by a member whose `config.author` covers all entities and approved by one whose `config.approve` does; its request is not shown to an approver of named entities. A policy version of an entity is that entity's: the author's permission covers it, and so does the approver's. A member whose role names entities authors and approves the versions of those entities and is refused elsewhere (403) with the sentence that a member of all entities, or of that entity, authors it. Rev 1.207 (04 API-C-03 rev 1.319): the version of another ENTITY is answered to her as a version that does not exist; the sentence is the answer for a workspace or book version. Which roles hold the two permissions does not change (ACT-22, §2.5); whether a settings category should rather be the Tenant Admin's (ACT-44, BR-AI-02) is not decided by this rule. |

## 4. Journeys

Every journey lists its tenant, preconditions, personas and the requirements and controls it proves. Steps name the actor (user or API client), the surface, the action and the expected system behaviour. Acceptance items are exact. Alternate paths are part of the same spec unless marked otherwise.

## 5. Module behaviour specifications

### 5.1 Common interaction rules

| Id | Rule | Rationale [J] / authority |
|---|---|---|
| BR-PLT-01 | Tenants are created only by a platform operator (REQ-PLT-038), through the operator command `erev tenant create --code <code> --name <display name> --reporting-currency <ISO 4217> [--demo] --admin <email>` or the operator route `POST /operator/tenants` (04 API-R-54). Both run 04 §14.3 `tenant.provision` in one transaction. It creates: the tenant (kind `production`, `is_demo` from `--demo`) with its audit HMAC key reference; the admin user when the email is unknown; a membership `INVITED` with an invitation (T-PLT-07); a Tenant Admin role assignment approved by rule `AUTO-BOOTSTRAP`; and the invitation email. An existing code is refused with 422 `validation-failed`, `errors[0].rule_id = "TENANT_CODE_EXISTS"`, and nothing is created. Until the invited Tenant Admin accepts, the operator can issue that invitation again with `erev tenant resend-invitation --code <code> --admin <email>` (rev 1.43; 04 §14.3 step 5): a new link replaces the earlier one, the 7 days start again and the email is sent again; an accepted or removed membership, and an invitation a Tenant Admin sent, are refused and nothing changes. There is no self-service sign-up in 1.0. Sandboxes are created only through REQ-PLT-023 and REQ-FC-001. | No hosted billing or entitlement exists in 1.0 (§6.5 of the register); REQ-PLT-038; D-75 Q9 (§9 Q3) |
| BR-PLT-02 | Until setup is completed, role assignments by the bootstrap Tenant Admin are approved by rule `AUTO-BOOTSTRAP`, recorded with that rule id and listed as "Setup grants" in the access listing and the next access review. Setup completes when at least one entity with an open period exists and the permissions to prepare and to approve contracts are held by two different users. Completion ends the rule. Rev 1.33 (supervisor ruling R-38 (iv) and its addendum): the bootstrap Tenant Admin is the user of the role assignment that provisioning created, not every holder of the Tenant Admin role, and the rule also stops applying — whatever the setup state, and for good — once another person has been an active member holding the permission to approve the grant (supervisor rulings R-66 (2) and R-73): from then on every role assignment waits for a person, even when that approver is later suspended, removed or loses the role. Rev 1.126 (the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-01): "holding the permission to approve the grant" means for every legal entity the grant names. A grant another member could approve waits for a person; a grant no other member has ever been able to approve — the first other administrator is for one entity and the grant is for another, or for all entities — is still approved by the rule while setup is incomplete, so that a workspace is not left without a way to grant access for its other entities. No action that takes access away is refused in order to keep a second approver; a workspace left with one access approver is shown as such and repaired by the platform operator. Rev 1.183 (item SBX-EMPTY-BOOTSTRAP-1): an EMPTY sandbox is seeded as a new tenant is, and its seed is provisioning's own grant, so the user who asked for it is its bootstrap Tenant Admin: the rule approves that user's role assignments there until its setup completes or another person has been an active access approver. | A new tenant has no second approver; the grants stay reviewable |
| BR-PLT-03 | The UI generates one `Idempotency-Key` per form submission and sends `If-Match` for mutable records. A 412 response shows ERR-07 and a "Reload" action. | REQ-PLT-026, 027 |
| BR-PLT-04 | Approvable commands follow one pattern: preparer submits → request stores subject content hash and impact preview → steps activate in order → decisions → the subject command executes in the same transaction as the final decision, after re-checking the content hash. A mismatch voids the request (`STALE_SUBJECT`). | REQ-PLT-013 to 015 |
| BR-PLT-05 | A preparer may edit a submitted object. The edit voids the pending request with reason `STALE_SUBJECT`, notifies the assigned approvers (NTF-04) and returns the object to Draft for resubmission. | REQ-PLT-014 |
| BR-PLT-06 | Step-up MFA: approving any request, locking, reopening, approving access or SoD exceptions, creating API clients, enabling AI and creating or revoking an approval delegation (rev 1.37; BR-PLT-07) require a TOTP verification within the last 5 minutes; otherwise the UI asks for a code (ERR-28). Other actions rely on the session's MFA. | Approvals are the controls auditors test; D-44 |
| BR-PLT-07 | Delegation (T-PLT-21) lets an approver delegate approval permissions for at most 90 days. A delegate must still differ from the preparer and from earlier approvers; decisions show "on behalf of <delegator>". Rev 1.33: a delegated decision relies on ONE delegation whose delegator still holds the permission for every legal entity of the request (04 §16.10 "Entity scope of a request"), and neither the delegate nor the delegator may be a person the subject excludes (the runner of a journal run, BR-JE-01; the owner of a waived item; the author of a judgement record or of a combination proposal). A delegation conveys the permission, not the view (supervisor ruling R-41): the delegate decides, sees in the queue and is notified of a request only when the delegate READS its subject in full in her own right — one permission that reads it, for every legal entity of the request (rev 1.207; 04 §16.10 "Who reads a subject in full"; until then: only when the delegate's own entity scope covered every legal entity of the request); a delegate of several approvers decides on behalf of one who may decide the request. Giving approval authority away and taking it back are access administration (rev 1.37): creating and revoking a delegation need the delegator's MFA-verified session and the step-up of BR-PLT-06, a delegation names only approval permissions the delegator holds and another active member, never the delegator, and both commands are audited. The command enforces the 90 days and these limits whoever calls it. **Limits (rev 1.118; supervisor rulings R-111 (2) and (4)).** A delegation ends no later than 90 days after the command that creates it, whatever day it starts: no command gives the days beyond. It goes only to a member who has set up multi-factor authentication, and a delegate is held to the second factor like a holder of the permission (REQ-PLT-005). It is checked for separation of duties as a role assignment is — what the delegate holds, with the delegated permissions, must not meet an SoD rule that no approved exception covers (ERR-22) — and a permission held through a delegation counts when a role is assigned to the delegate or changed afterwards, from the day the delegation is given, whether or not it has started. A delegation ends — it does not wait — when its delegator stops being an active member or loses a permission it names, or — rev 1.201 (item SBX-COPY-OPEN-INVITATION-1) — when its delegate is removed from the workspace: what was delegated to a member ends with the removal, as what the member delegated does, because a removed person is invited again on the same membership (SM-13) and nothing granted before the removal passes into that second life; a suspended delegate keeps what was delegated, as a suspended member keeps the roles. A delegation that has ended never returns. Besides the delegator, an access administrator — a holder of `role.manage` for every entity of the delegator's roles — ends any delegation, with the step-up of BR-PLT-06. A refusal of either command for want of the second factor or of the step-up is audited. | REQ-PLT-005, 010, 011, 012, 013 |
| BR-PLT-08 | High-risk commands require a comment of at least 10 characters: lock, reopen, void, reject, cancel journal run, waive or dismiss an exception, apply or release a hold, request an SoD exception, end soft close. | DS-CMP-11; research 07 §5.3 |
| BR-PLT-09 | Commands expected to exceed 2 seconds return a job (REQ-PLT-029). The UI shows progress in place, allows navigation away and notifies on failure (NTF-05). | REQ-UX-021 |
| BR-PLT-10 | "As locked" reads use the lock snapshot id; "Known at" is an advanced read option on reports and the workbench (REQ-PLT-030). | D-19 |

### 5.2 State machines

Each state is a `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` §3 literal of the enum named in the heading, written in the enum's exact case. A state that is a behaviour (calculating, submitted, exporting, partially acknowledged, combined, quarantined, requested) is not an enum value: it maps to the `job` (T-PLT-27, E-13) that performs it, to an open `approval_request` (T-PLT-17, E-05) on the subject, or to a child row's own state, per the 04 §0.5 mapping rule and rows SMAP-01 to SMAP-15. Quoted labels such as "Calculated" are UI copy, never stored values (D-73). "Permission" is the T-PLT-11 code; "Effects" lists audit, postings and notifications. Every transition writes an audit event (REQ-PLT-019).

**SM-01 Approval request and steps (E-05, E-06)**

| From | To | Trigger (UI label) | Permission | Guard | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | `PENDING` | Submit for approval | subject command permission | Subject valid; preview generated for revenue-affecting subjects | First step `ACTIVE`; NTF-01 |
| `PENDING` | `PENDING` (next step) | Approve | step `required_permission` | Approver ≠ preparer and ≠ earlier approvers; step-up MFA; content hash unchanged | Decision stored with preview hash; next step `ACTIVE`; NTF-01 |
| `PENDING` | `APPROVED` | Approve (final step) | step permission | As above; `min_approvers` reached | Subject command executes (BR-PLT-04); NTF-02 |
| `PENDING` | `REJECTED` | Reject | step permission | Comment required | Subject returns to Draft; NTF-03 |
| `PENDING` | `VOIDED` | none (system) | none | Subject changed (`STALE_SUBJECT`) or voided (`SUBJECT_VOIDED`) | NTF-04 |
| `PENDING` | `WITHDRAWN` | Withdraw | preparer | No final decision yet | Subject returns to Draft; NTF-04 to assigned approvers |
| none | `APPROVED` | none (system) | none | Published auto-approval rule matches (REQ-PLT-016) | Decision `AUTO_APPROVE` names the rule id and version |

**SM-02 Contract status (E-17)**

| From | To | Trigger | Permission | Guard | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | `DRAFT` | Create contract; import; integration; accept AI proposal | `contract.create` | Required header fields | `CONTRACT_BOOKED` event |
| `DRAFT` | `PENDING_REVIEW` | Submit for activation | `contract.create` | Activation checklist passes (REQ-CON-005); otherwise ERR-31 | Request `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` or auto-approval |
| `PENDING_REVIEW` | `ACTIVE` | Approval executes | `contract.approve` | SM-01 | `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`, behind `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` for each book that moves when the contract was `NOT_A_CONTRACT` (rev 1.34); schedules computed; field locks on (REQ-CON-007) |
| `PENDING_REVIEW` | `DRAFT` | Reject or withdraw | step permission / preparer | none | none |
| `DRAFT` | `NOT_A_CONTRACT` | Record Step 1 "criteria not met" | `contract.create`, `judgement.create` | Judgement `NOT_A_CONTRACT` reviewed (rev 1.79; supervisor ruling R-102 (b): an assessment cites a reviewed judgement record — before it, "submitted"); every book the contract is computed in is kept by the contracting entity and assessed not probable (rev 1.34; supervisor ruling R-20; 04 §16.3). Otherwise the assessment is recorded and the contract stays `DRAFT` until its activation is approved. Once the contract has an activation event, an assessment or a significant-change flag is not dated before the latest Step 1 event it acts on — history is not rewritten by back-dating — and none is ever dated after the later of today and the contract's inception date (supervisor ruling R-84). Neither an assessment nor an activation is voided as an event: before activation, and behind the not-a-contract status, a later assessment replaces an assessment; on an active contract a reassessment that followed a significant-change flag is undone by the approved void of the flag; an activation is reversed by voiding the contract. An assessment or a flag is recorded in a request of its own, not inside an attribute-change request (rev 1.55, rev 1.67; supervisor rulings R-61 (b), R-77 (1); 04 §16.3) | Receipts post to `DEPOSIT_LIABILITY` (REQ-CON-003) |
| `NOT_A_CONTRACT` | `PENDING_REVIEW` | Record criteria met, then submit for activation (rev 1.34; supervisor ruling R-20) | `contract.create` (+ `judgement.create`) | Activation checklist passes (REQ-CON-005), including the criteria-met re-assessment for each book that moves, resting on a collectibility judgement reviewed after the contract was assessed not a contract (rev 1.67; supervisor ruling R-77 (3)); otherwise ERR-31 | Request `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` with the steps of any activation (§2.5), never auto-approved whatever the contract's origin, amount and flags (rev 1.55; supervisor ruling R-61 (c)); the preview and the approval request show the books that move and the catch-up. On approval: `ACTIVE` with catch-up (POL-013). A rejected, withdrawn or voided request leaves the contract `NOT_A_CONTRACT` |
| `ACTIVE` | `ACTIVE` (unchanged; the book is not a contract) | Record a not-probable reassessment after a significant change (rev 1.138; item STEP1-HOLD-RELEASE-1) | `event.record`, `judgement.create` | A significant-change flag stands that no assessment of the book has followed — recorded before, or in the same request — otherwise IMP-136: without the flag the assessment would be recorded and change nothing (606-10-25-5). Judgement `NOT_A_CONTRACT` reviewed | The book is not a contract from the assessment's date while the contract's status stays `ACTIVE`; the recognition hold of the judgement record, which its review did not release, is released with the assessment (SM-10); the way back is the row below |
| `ACTIVE` | `ACTIVE` (unchanged) | Record criteria met for a book that is not a contract, then submit for activation (rev 1.79; supervisor ruling R-102 (a)) | `contract.create` (+ `judgement.create`) | A book of the contract is not a contract as not probable — the books were assessed differently when the contract was activated, or a reassessment after a significant change took the book out — and has its criteria-met re-assessment, resting on a collectibility judgement reviewed after the book was put out; the activation checklist passes; otherwise ERR-31. A book that is not a contract for another reason is refused by name (IMP-133; a known limitation) | Request `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` with the steps of any activation (§2.5), never auto-approved; the preview and the approval request show the books that move and the catch-up. On approval: `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` for each such book and no `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`; the contract's status does not change; catch-up (POL-013). A rejected, withdrawn or voided request changes nothing |
| `ACTIVE` | `COMPLETED` | none (system) | none | Every obligation `SATISFIED` or `CANCELLED` | none |
| `COMPLETED` | `ACTIVE` | none (system) | none | A later event changes remaining allocation (return, price change, modification) [J] | none |
| `ACTIVE` | `TERMINATED` | Modification of kind `TERMINATION` applied | `modification.approve` | SM-03 | REQ-MOD-011 effects |

| any except `VOIDED`, for a contract without a current non-singleton group membership (SMAP-01) | `VOIDED` | Void contract (approved) | `contract.void` then `contract.approve` | Reason; routing §2.5 (1 step without posted lines, 2 steps with posted lines) | Reversal lines in the first open period with origin periods (REQ-CON-015) |

**SM-04 Estimate version, SSP book version and configuration version (E-12)**

| Object | Path | Guard and effects |
|---|---|---|
| Estimate version (VC, constraint, return rate, breakage, EAC, exercise likelihood, royalty accrual, attestation) | `DRAFT` → `SUBMITTED` → `APPROVED` (effective) → `SUPERSEDED` when a later version is approved; `SUBMITTED` → `REJECTED` → `DRAFT`; `SUBMITTED` → `WITHDRAWN` → `DRAFT`; `DRAFT` → `VOIDED` ("Discard draft", `estimate.create`; rev 1.139: a draft had one exit, its submission). At most one version of an element is `DRAFT` or `SUBMITTED`: a second one is refused where it would become open — at its creation, and at the edit that returns a `REJECTED` or `WITHDRAWN` version to `DRAFT` (ERR-93; rev 1.168) | Rationale required; evidence required for VC, EAC and return-rate versions — an attachment whose file can still be read, asked at the submission (IMP-138) and again at the approval (ERR-94); a VC version whose values equal the approved version's is an attestation of no change instead: it owes no file and states its reason in at least 10 characters (IMP-139), and a version that sends the attestation flag with other values is refused (IMP-141); a VC version names the `CONSTRAINT` judgement record of its element at the submission (IMP-140) and is approved once that record is reviewed (ERR-94; rev 1.168); method fixed after version 1 (POL-040); approval executes `ESTIMATE_CHANGED` and records the version pair on the catch-up (D-20). The request pins the contract's group and head: once the contract has changed — an event appended to it or to a member of its group, a regroup — a decision on the request, approval or rejection, answers 409 `stale-approval`, the request is voided and the version is `WITHDRAWN`; its preparer edits it back to a draft and submits it again with a new preview (rev 1.165; 04 §16.10 rev 1.233) |
| SSP book version | `DRAFT` → `SUBMITTED` → `APPROVED` → `SUPERSEDED`; `REJECTED` and `WITHDRAWN` return to `DRAFT` | Attachment and methodology label before submission, the attachment — a file that can still be read — also at approval (ERR-10; rev 1.145); no overlap per scope (ERR-32); second approver per REQ-SSP-007; supersession per BR-SSP-02 |
| Configuration (rule sets, templates, account mappings, policy set, approval matrices, SoD rules, roles, mapping profiles, practical-expedient flags, holds policies) | `DRAFT` → `TESTED` (attached example cases pass) → `SUBMITTED` → `APPROVED` → `PUBLISHED` (effective date) → `SUPERSEDED`; `REJECTED` and `WITHDRAWN` return to `DRAFT` — a policy version only while the version it was submitted on is still the published one of its category and scope (ERR-92; rev 1.112) | Simulation attached before approval (REQ-POL-006); prospective effect (REQ-POL-007); approver ≠ author (SoD-5) |

**SM-08 Journal run and journal batch (E-34, E-13, E-05; export state on `journal_batch`, SMAP-09)**

| From | To | Trigger | Permission | Guard | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | none (job `QUEUED` then `RUNNING`; label "Calculating") | Run journals (or close run step) | `journal.run` | Period `open`, `closing` or `reopened` (E-04); a period that is `closed` or `permanently_locked` answers 409 `period-closed` (ERR-86; rev 1.134), and a `future` period 422 `validation-failed` on `period_key` — "<period key> is not open yet for <entity> in book <book>. Open the period before running journals." — at the command: this guard was stated here and not enforced until rev 1.134. Rev 1.174 (item JRN-EMPTY-RUN-1): and the period has no run that is not cancelled, or the run would hold a line — beside such a run a further run without a line is not made: the command answers 409 `invalid-transition` and starts no job (ERR-96). Rev 1.184 (item JRN-HELD-AFTER-EXPORT-1): a line that an earlier run left out under a journal-export hold released since is such a line — the run takes it over, whether or not the earlier run has been exported (04 T-SL-06 rev 1.267) | Job `JOURNAL_RUN_CALCULATE` enqueued (SMAP-05) |
| none | `draft` (label "Calculated") | none (system) | none | Job `JOURNAL_RUN_CALCULATE` `SUCCEEDED` | Run and batches inserted; batches balanced per entity, book, currency, period (REQ-JE-001). The first run of a period without activity is inserted without a batch or a line: it is the period's run as it stands, the lock gates count it, and it is never submitted (rev 1.174) |
| none | none (job `FAILED`; label "Calculation failed") | none (system) | none | Validation failure (`ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING`, `JOURNAL_UNBALANCED`), or a period that was closed before the job ran (ERR-86; rev 1.134), or a run of the same entity, book and period that was made between the command and the job and left this one no line (ERR-96; rev 1.174) | No run row; the job's `problem` and exception items (source `JOURNAL`) carry the codes (SMAP-05); NTF-05 |
| `draft` | `draft` (label "Submitted") | Submit | `journal.run` | The run has at least one journal line: a run without lines answers 409 `invalid-transition`, "Journal run <run> has no journal lines: there is nothing to approve." (rev 1.174; item JRN-EMPTY-RUN-1; 04 rev 1.248) | Request `JOURNAL_RUN` `PENDING` (SMAP-06); NTF-01 |
| `draft` | `approved` | Approve (final step) | `journal.approve` | Approver ≠ runner (BR-JE-01); SM-01 | Request `APPROVED`; outbox intent written on approval (REQ-JE-013) |
| `approved` | `approved` (label "Exporting") | Export | `journal.export` | Tenant kind `production` (REQ-PLT-022), otherwise 403 `sandbox-restricted` | Job `JOURNAL_EXPORT` `QUEUED` then `RUNNING` (SMAP-07); chunks sent with deterministic external ids |
| `approved` | `exported` | none (system) | none | Job `JOURNAL_EXPORT` `SUCCEEDED`; every batch `exported` | none |
| `exported` | `acknowledged` or `failed` | Adapter receipt per batch; or "Record ERP reference" for CSV (BR-JE-03) | system / `journal.export` | Each batch changes state on its receipt (E-36). The run rolls up in the same transaction: `acknowledged` (with `acknowledged_at`) when every batch is `acknowledged`; `failed` when at least one batch is `failed` and none is `exported`; otherwise it stays `exported`, and the UI shows "Partially acknowledged: <n> of <m> batches" (SMAP-08) | Posting receipts; NTF-10 on failure |
| `approved` | `failed` | none (system) | none | The adapter refuses a batch (`Permanent`), or the last attempt of the retry schedule fails (05 ADP-12), before anything was exported | The batch passes `exported` inside the same transaction (E-34 has no pair `approved` → `failed`); that leg is not observable: no export time, file, notification or webhook. A `REJECTED` receipt carries the adapter's message; exception item `JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED` (IMP-130); NTF-10; the run rolls up (SMAP-08) (rev 1.56) |
| `failed` | `exported` | Retry export ("Retry" on the failed batch; `POST /journal-batches/{id}/retry`), or "Retry export" on the run — `POST /journal-runs/{id}/export`, which retries every failed batch of the run under the same rule (rev 1.172; item JRN-RUN-RETRY-1; 04 rev 1.244): beside a failed batch that is being sent the run's command is refused whole, 409 by that batch's name, and writes nothing for any batch | `journal.export` | A failed batch is re-sent. Not while it is being sent (rev 1.167; 04 §16.7 `retry` rev 1.221; item JRN-RETRY-CLAIMED-1): while the batch's export message is claimed the command answers 409 `invalid-transition` — "Batch <batch> is being sent. It cannot be retried while it is being sent. An interrupted export resumes about 15 minutes after it stopped." — and writes nothing: the claimed message is the retry | Run returns to `exported` (SMAP-08); retries do not duplicate postings (BR-JE-02); the batch's `JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED` item is resolved (rev 1.56). Rev 1.167: while the batch's export message is still to be sent, or failed and is due again, the command answers 202 with its job and writes no second message — that message is the retry, and the job dispatches it when it is due; the job's `result.waiting` names each batch whose message is due again, with the instant from which it is sent next. The retry, the hand-over and the acknowledgement of a batch are taken in a closed period too: a period can lock with a batch `failed` or `exported` under an approved waiver of the lock gate "batches acknowledged" (BR-CLS-01), and the three commands complete the export of the population the lock certified — where a run is neither calculated nor cancelled (ERR-86) |
| `failed` | `exported` | Hand over for manual posting (`POST /journal-batches/{id}/hand-over`) | `journal.export` | A failed batch of an ERP adapter; the ledger is asked first and does not hold it; the batch's lines are those approved (the recount); and the ledger has had its time (rev 1.173; item JRN-EXIT-SETTLE-1; 04 rev 1.247): 409 `invalid-transition`, at the command and in the job, for 15 minutes after the batch's last attempt ended without the ledger's refusal — the answer names the minutes left — and at any age for a batch the ledger accepted and does not show yet, which leaves by a retry alone | The batch's file is stored and the batch waits for "Record ERP reference" (BR-JE-03) as a CSV batch does; no message, no receipt; the run returns to `exported`; the batch's `JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED` item is resolved (rev 1.88; item JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1) |
| `failed` | `cancelled` | Cancel journal run | `journal.run` | Every batch `failed` or `approved` — a run with a batch `exported` or `acknowledged` is refused (ERR-74); the latest run of its entity, book and period; no batch being sent or still to be sent; and the ledger, asked by the job the command starts, holds none of its failed batches — a batch it holds is acknowledged instead and the cancel refused (ERR-73); the ledger has had its time (rev 1.173; item JRN-EXIT-SETTLE-1; 04 rev 1.247) — the cancel answers 409 `invalid-transition`, at the command and in the job, for 15 minutes after a failed batch's last attempt ended without the ledger's refusal, naming the minutes left, and at any age while the ledger accepted a batch and does not show it yet: that batch leaves by a retry alone; an unreachable ledger changes nothing; and the period is not `closed` or `permanently_locked` — otherwise 409 `period-closed`, at the command and in the job (ERR-86; rev 1.134) | Run and batches `cancelled`; the batches' `JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED` items resolved with the cancel's reason; the period is calculated again as a new `draft` run with new external ids (rev 1.88; item JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1; supervisor ruling R-112 (c) of 2026-09-30) |
| `draft`, `approved` | `cancelled` | Cancel journal run | `journal.run` | Not exported; comment (BR-PLT-08); the latest non-cancelled run of its entity, book and period — an earlier run is refused with "Journal run <run> was calculated after this run for the same entity, book and period. Cancel <run> first."; no batch of the run is being sent — otherwise "An export of this journal run is in progress: batch <batch> is being sent. It cannot be cancelled while a batch is being sent. An interrupted export resumes about 15 minutes after it stopped."; no batch of the run waits to be sent again after a failed attempt — otherwise "An export of this journal run is in progress: an attempt to send batch <batch> failed and it will be sent again. It cannot be cancelled until the batch has been sent or has failed."; the period is not `closed` or `permanently_locked` — otherwise 409 `period-closed`, "<period> is closed for <entity> in book <book>. A journal run of a closed period cannot be cancelled." (ERR-86; rev 1.134) (04 §16.7; rev 1.56, the third rev 1.133) | A pending `JOURNAL_RUN` request is voided (`SUBJECT_VOIDED`) |
