**SM-10 Manual adjustment (E-94) and judgement record (E-57)**

| Object | Path | Guard and effects |
|---|---|---|
| Manual adjustment | `DRAFT` → `SUBMITTED` → `APPROVED` → `POSTED`; `SUBMITTED` → `REJECTED`; `SUBMITTED` → `DRAFT` (the preparer withdraws the request, or the request is voided as stale); `REJECTED` → `DRAFT` (revise and resubmit); `DRAFT` → `VOIDED` (discarded with a reason); `POSTED` → `VOIDED` through an approved reversal | Reason code; attachment above threshold (§2.5) — a document whose file can still be read; an approved shred of it makes a pending request stale (rev 1.145); pending items (`DRAFT`, `SUBMITTED`) block lock unless deferred with approval (REQ-JE-019): the deferral is an approval request of the same adjustment that replaces its pending posting request on the same routing rows — approved, the adjustment stays `SUBMITTED`, is marked deferred past lock and no longer blocks; rejected, it stays `SUBMITTED` and blocks; a later submit routes it for posting, and an approval after the lock posts in the first open period with the adjustment's period as origin; a deferral ends when the adjustment returns to `DRAFT`; `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_APPLIED` event on posting (rev 1.49; supervisor ruling R-51). Rev 1.87 (item ADJ-VOID-REFUSE-1; supervisor ruling R-112 (b) of 2026-09-30): the path `POSTED` → `VOIDED` has no command in 1.0 — a known limitation: a posted adjustment is corrected by a further adjustment, and the void of its `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_APPLIED` event is refused, because the event voided beside a row that stays `POSTED` would be two truths (ERR-70). A `DRAFT` or `REJECTED` adjustment is edited only by the person who created it, so the self-approval rule cannot be stepped around by a second editor (ERR-71); submit and discard stay open to every holder of the preparing permission, and the creator and the submitter are both excluded from approving |
| Judgement record | `DRAFT` → `SUBMITTED` → `REVIEWED`; `SUBMITTED` → `REJECTED` → `DRAFT`; `REVIEWED` → `SUPERSEDED` by a later record on the same topic; `DRAFT` → `VOIDED` ("Discard", `judgement.create`; rev 1.169: a draft had one exit, its submission — the draft proposal of a combination group excepted, which is decided with its group; rev 1.196: it is voided with its group when the proposal is discarded (ACT-04), and is edited by nobody); "Discard" takes a `REJECTED` record too (rev 1.199; item JDG-REJECTED-EXIT-1: a rejected record had one exit, its creator's edit) — the command takes it `REJECTED` → `DRAFT` → `VOIDED` in one unit of work, the two pairs of this row, and no pair is added | Reviewer ≠ preparer; an unreviewed record affecting an open period blocks lock (REQ-POL-008). A record holds the active contract it names from its submission until it is reviewed, whatever its subject — but a record of a modification or of an estimate version holds nothing, because its subject changes nothing before its own approval; a rejected, withdrawn or stale-voided review leaves the hold. A `NOT_A_CONTRACT` record holds until the not-probable assessment that cites it is recorded, until it is superseded, or until a `COLLECTIBILITY` record of the same subject and book is reviewed after it (rev 1.138; REQ-POL-010; 04 T-CON-20) |

**SM-11 AI proposal (E-74, E-05; REQ-AI-003)**

| From | To | Trigger | Permission | Guard | Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | `proposed` | Review a document; Explain narrative; anomaly summary; Q&A | `ai.use` | AI enabled (BR-AI-03), otherwise 409 `ai-disabled` | Call log (REQ-AI-004) |
| none | `failed` | none (system) | none | Provider error, provider refusal (`AI_REFUSAL`), timeout (NFR-09) or budget stop | `problem.code` stored; nothing else written (REQ-AI-010) |
| `proposed` | `accepted` | Create draft or run command with accepted fields | `ai.use` plus the command's permission | At least one field accepted | Normal command by the accepting user; flag `AI_ASSISTED` |
| `proposed` | `proposed` (label "Awaiting approval") | Accept into an existing record under approval | as above | Request `AI_PROPOSAL_ACCEPTANCE` `PENDING` (SMAP-11; routing §2.5) | none until the request is decided |
| `proposed` | `accepted` | none (system) | none | Request `AI_PROPOSAL_ACCEPTANCE` `APPROVED` | Accepted fields applied |
| `proposed` | `rejected` | Reject proposal | `ai.use` | Reason | none |
| `proposed` | `expired` | none (system) | none | 30 days without action (BR-AI-04) | none |

**SM-12 Migration batch (E-76)**

| Path | Guard and effects |
|---|---|
| `UPLOADED` → `PROFILING` → `PROFILED` → `IMPORTING` → `IMPORTED` → `RECONCILED` → `SUBMITTED` → `PROMOTED`; any stage → `FAILED` or `CANCELLED` | Source opened from a copy; SHA-256 recorded; duplicate refused (BR-MIG-01); promotion requires `migration.approve` by a user other than the runner and unexplained differences 0 (BR-MIG-02) |

**SM-13 Membership, role assignment and SoD exception (E-78, E-96)**

| Object | Path | Guard and effects |
|---|---|---|
| Membership | `INVITED` → `ACTIVE` (invitation accepted) → `SUSPENDED` → `ACTIVE`; any → `REMOVED`; `REMOVED` → `INVITED` (invited again; rev 1.201) | Suspension ends sessions immediately; removal keeps history. Rev 1.201 (item SBX-COPY-OPEN-INVITATION-1): a person who was removed from a workspace — a member, or a person whose invitation was withdrawn — is invited again like anyone else, and the invitation is issued on the membership the person had: one membership, one history, one audit trail. The roles of before stay revoked, and the invitation's roles are requested as for any invitation. Nothing asked or granted for the membership before the removal passes into its second life: a role request or an SoD exception request still waiting is voided, and an approved SoD exception still in force is revoked — whoever invites, and whatever legal entity a request names; and what was delegated to the member ended with the removal (BR-PLT-07). A person who is invited, active or suspended in the workspace is refused as before, as already a member; so is the identity an erasure left (05 PRV-07), whose membership stays removed — the person's own address is then a new person's |
| Role assignment | requested → active → revoked (no status column; SMAP-14) | Requested = request `ROLE_ASSIGNMENT` `PENDING` with no `role_assignment` row; active = `role_assignment` row with `revoked_at IS NULL`; revoked = `revoked_at IS NOT NULL` (T-PLT-10). SoD check at request and at approval (REQ-PLT-010); entity scope stored |
| SoD exception | `REQUESTED` → `APPROVED` → `EXPIRED` or `REVOKED`; `REQUESTED` → `REJECTED` | Compensating control text; validity ≤ 366 days (T-PLT-14); listed in the SoD report |

**SM-14 Close run (E-62), sync run (E-72), hold (E-45) and scenario (E-100)**

| Object | Path | Guard and effects |
|---|---|---|
| Close run | `PENDING` → `RUNNING` → `SUCCEEDED`; `RUNNING` → `BLOCKED` (quarantined contracts or failed invariant) → `RUNNING` (resume); `RUNNING` → `FAILED` or `CANCELLED` | Steps of REQ-CLS-012 recorded with timings; resumable from the failed step |
| Sync run | `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` → `SUCCEEDED`, `CONTROL_TOTAL_MISMATCH` or `FAILED` | Mismatch raises an exception, notifies the integration owner and blocks lock (REQ-DAT-010) |
| Hold | applied → released (no status enum; SMAP-15) | Events `HOLD_APPLIED` and `HOLD_RELEASED` (E-03) and `contract_hold` rows (T-CON-20); hold type E-45 `recognition` or `journal_export`. Comment on both; system holds release automatically when their condition clears, with actor `SYSTEM` (REQ-POL-010). The hold of a judgement record is not released by hand while the record waits for its review, nor — for a `NOT_A_CONTRACT` record — after the review, where the assessment that cites the record releases it (rev 1.138; 04 §16.1 `release-hold`) |
| Scenario | `ACTIVE` → `ARCHIVED` | Archived scenarios are read-only; archiving by the creator or a Controller |

### 5.3 Business rules

| Id | Rule | REQ |
|---|---|---|
| BR-REF-01 | An entity's functional currency and time zone show a lock icon with the tooltip "Fixed after the first posting." once any journal line exists. | REQ-REF-001 |
| BR-REF-02 | A calendar change applies only to future periods with no postings; the setup screen lists the affected periods before saving. | REQ-REF-002 |
| BR-SSP-01 | An approved SSP book version is read-only in every channel (ERR-09). | REQ-SSP-001 |
| BR-SSP-02 | Approving a version whose effective-from falls inside an approved version of the same scope sets the earlier version's effective-to to the day before, once, as an audited supersession transition. Values and effective-from never change. Any other overlap is refused (ERR-32). | REQ-SSP-001 |
| BR-SSP-03 | A per-obligation SSP override needs a justification and an `SSP_OVERRIDE` approval and appears in the SSP override listing. | REQ-SSP-006 |
| BR-SSP-04 | The historical SSP calculator accepts an uploaded CSV of standalone sales or committed contract lines as its pool. The pool file is stored with its SHA-256, posts nothing and needs no approval; its output is a Draft version. | REQ-SSP-009 |
| BR-SSP-05 | Publishing checks POL-073 range statistics and shows warnings without blocking when the mode is `WARN`. | REQ-SSP-005 |
| BR-POL-01 | A policy or configuration change cannot be submitted without a simulation report; the report states "No contracts affected" when that is the result. | REQ-POL-006 |
| BR-POL-02 | Applying the legacy-parity preset creates a policy version with the POLICIES.md `Parity` values and goes through SM-04 configuration approval. | REQ-POL-005 |
| BR-POL-03 | Industry policy templates are delivered as Draft configuration and cannot be used until tested and approved. | REQ-POL-009 |
| BR-CON-01 | The activation checklist lists, per item: mandatory fields; source reference; product, template and SSP resolution for every line; distinct review per obligation; open combination suggestions; required judgement records; Step 1 record. Each failing item links to the place to fix it. The Step 1 record of a draft contract (rev 1.67; supervisor ruling R-77 (2), (7)): every book of the contracting entity has a reviewed assessment of that book, and the assessment in force at the contract's inception date — where the activation takes effect — is the book's latest. Replacing a draft (rev 1.79; supervisor ruling R-102 (c)) voids its Step 1 assessments with the booking they judged: the item fails until a new Step 1 review is recorded, reviewed and assessed, and a review made before the replacement is not cited again. A Step 1 review (rev 1.79; supervisor ruling R-113 (f)) records the five criteria of ASC 606-10-25-1; a review that answers No to "Approved and committed", "Rights identified" or "Payment terms identified" does not carry an activation: the item fails by name (IMP-135) and the contract stays a draft. A probable assessment likewise does not rest on a review that answers No to "Collectibility probable": it is refused by the same name (rev 1.138). | REQ-CON-005 |
| BR-CON-02 | After activation, stated prices, quantities, dates, accounts, entity and SSP version are read-only (ERR-11). Memos, custom attributes and dimension values stay editable with a mandatory comment. | REQ-CON-007, 008 |
| BR-CON-03 | Combination suggestions appear on both contracts and in the exception queue as an exception item with code `COMBINATION_SUGGESTED` (severity `WARNING`, raised as X `ENGINE`, disposition `remediable`; 04 §15.4, table placement by 04; copy IMP-89). A suggestion is resolved by combining (approved) or by dismissing it with a rationale (§9 Q10, resolved by D-76; D-73). A contract assessed not a contract (rev 1.67; supervisor ruling R-77 (5)) is not combined into a group that is computed in a book its own entity does not keep; it is combined after it is activated. Rev 1.181 (item ACT-CHECKLIST-SUGGESTION-SCOPE-1; 04 table 15.4-I): "on both contracts" is the activation checklist of each — one line for each open suggestion (IMP-103), in the checklist and in the refusal of the activation's submission. A suggestion to combine contracts of two contracting entities is listed for, combined and dismissed by a member who reads every contract it names (04 T-IMP-05; item EXC-IMPORT-SCOPE-1). The reader of one of its contracts keeps the line, because an open suggestion blocks the activation of the contract she reads, and is told "a contract outside your entities" where the other contract's external id would stand; she asks a member who reads both to combine the contracts or to dismiss the suggestion. A line that is stored — the message of the item a Contract Setup commit raises when the checklist of a contract fails (BR-DAT-06) — is written for all its readers: it names the other contract only when that contract is of the contract's own contracting entity and says "a contract of another entity" otherwise. | REQ-CON-010 |
| BR-MOD-01 | The questionnaire pre-fills answers the system can compute (distinct from the template; "priced at SSP" from the modification-date range, POL-101). The preparer confirms every answer. A chosen treatment that differs from the proposal requires a judgement record and adds flag `TREATMENT_OVERRIDE` to the request. | REQ-MOD-002 |
| BR-MOD-02 | Estimate versions and judgement records created inside a modification are linked to it; each has its own approval request, and the approvals inbox groups them for bulk approval. The modification applies only when every linked item is approved. Rev 1.139 (supervisor rulings R-118 (e) and R-119 (e); 04 rev 1.210): an estimate version names its modification from its creation on. The linked versions are approved before the modification is submitted — the approval of a version moves the contract's head, which a modification's request pins — and the submission and the approval of the modification refuse by name while one is not approved (ERR-87). The link orders two approvals and moves no figure: a linked version posts its own change at its own effective date. The grouping of the linked requests in the inbox is not built. Rev 1.169 (the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-01; 04 rev 1.242): a judgement record whose subject is the modification is reviewed, or discarded, before the modification is submitted — its review request pins the contract's head, which the applied modification moves, so a record that waits could never be reviewed afterwards — and the submission and the approval refuse by name while one is draft or waiting for review (ERR-95). | REQ-MOD-001; REQ-PLT-017 |
| BR-MOD-03 | A legacy Contract Modification upload requires a mode (`prospective`, `retrospective`, `pob_price_change`) and an effective date; under native policies the mode is the proposed treatment. | REQ-DAT-003 |
| BR-MOD-04 | The preview of a backdated modification shows the replay branch point and, where the effective date is in a closed period, the posting period and origin period. | REQ-MOD-018 |
| BR-TP-01 | The estimation method of a VC element is fixed after version 1 (POL-040); the UI shows it disabled with a tooltip. | REQ-TP-002 |
| BR-TP-02 | For each open VC element, a version or an approved "No change" attestation effective at period end is a close gate (POL-042). | REQ-TP-004 |
| BR-REC-01 | Progress, acceptance, cost, return, usage (rev 1.166) and option-exercise events entered in the UI are tagged `MANUAL_UI` and need `MANUAL_EVENT` approval; events from integrations, API clients and approved imports do not. Entered by a signed-in person, such an event is not appended (rev 1.123): the request waits as an event submission until a user other than its preparer, holding `event.approve`, approves it; the approval appends the events — marked manual, each naming the request — and computes the contract. Other events sent in the same request (the billing beside a delivery, the credit memo beside a return) wait with it. A progress, milestone, cost or acceptance event carries at least one evidence file, which the approver finds on the request. After the approval the evidence is attached to each recorded event, where the readers of the contract open it (rev 1.185). A usage report may carry one — the usage statement — and is taken without it (rev 1.166). The preparer can withdraw the request; a rejected or withdrawn request appends nothing. No command records an option exercise in 1.0 (04 §16.3 "Manual events"). | REQ-DAT-014 A usage report states usage that has occurred (rev 1.208; item USAGE-REPORT-PERIOD-ENDED-1): usage is reported for a period that has ended. The usage period of a usage report or royalty statement ends on or before the report's date; one whose period ends later is refused where it is sent, by every channel, with IMP-148. A customer that reports inside a month reports the usage up to the day of the report. |
| BR-DAT-01 | Imports are all-or-nothing unless `quarantine_failed_rows` is on. | REQ-DAT-008 |
| BR-DAT-02 | `ERROR` findings block the commit; `WARNING` findings are shown in the dry-run diff and logged. The exception severity follows E-43 (`ERROR` → `BLOCKING`, `WARNING` → `WARNING`). | 04 §15.4; DEVIATIONS §5; D-30a |
| BR-DAT-03 | A file with the same tenant, template version and SHA-256 as a committed import is refused at upload with 409 `duplicate-import` and `errors[0].rule_id = "IMPORT_FILE_DUPLICATE"` (IMP-05); no import is created. | REQ-DAT-001; 04 §15.2 |
| BR-DAT-04 | An exception item may be dismissed without approval only when its input was never committed (a rejected import, a discarded sync notification) and a comment names why no accounting effect is pending. Every other clearance by a person is a waiver: a request of subject `EXCEPTION_WAIVER` approved with `exception.waive` (SM-06). The system resolves an item whose condition it finds cleared: after a reprocess, and for a data-quality finding that the monitors no longer make (SM-06; rev 1.114). Rev 1.157 (item JOB-FAILED-ITEM-1): an item `JOB_FAILED` — the record of a job that failed, raised as information — is dismissed the same way, with a comment: its dismissal discards no input, and what the job should have produced is watched by the close blockers (03 REQ-OPS-006). The system resolves it when a later job of the same kind for the same record completes. Rev 1.197 (item CLO-RATE-AFTER-RUN-1): an item `FX_RATE_CHANGED_AFTER_LOCK` — the approval of a rate set version changed an exchange rate dated in a period that is closed for an entity and book — is cleared by a person through the waiver alone: its input is committed, and the waiver is the recorded acceptance of the difference where the next open period's close run posts it. The system resolves it when the period it names is reopened: the period is no longer closed, and its next lock needs a close run that read the rates in force (04 T-REF-11 "A rate changed after a lock"). Until one of the two, it holds the lock of the period the difference posts to (REQ-CLS-009). | REQ-DAT-009 |
| BR-DAT-05 | The dry-run diff shows per contract: versions affected, allocation changes, schedule changes by period, balance changes, journal preview and findings. | REQ-DAT-015 |
| BR-DAT-06 | Approving a legacy v1 Contract Setup import activates each contract whose checklist passes; a contract that fails stays Draft with an exception item. The import approval is the activation approval for those contracts. | REQ-DAT-002; REQ-CON-005 |
| BR-DAT-07 | While a period is `closing`, imports and adapter loads affecting it wait for human review: auto-approval rules do not apply. | REQ-CLS-003 |
| BR-CLS-01 | Lock gates: interface batches complete; journal balancing; journal completeness; pending approvals affecting the period 0 (rev 1.205: the period's own lock request is not one — it asks for the lock, and J-13.14 decides it on the cockpit; 04 §16.8 `blockers` rev 1.311); exceptions resolved, waived or dismissed; batches acknowledged; reconciliations generated and reviewed or auto-certified; judgements reviewed; VC attestations (BR-TP-02); open holds released or waived; data-quality findings of severity `ERROR` cleared (04 table 15.4-E); custom close tasks signed; controller certification. Rev 1.35 (supervisor ruling R-55): a custom close task gates the lock when it is blocking (04 T-CLS-02 `is_blocking`); a gate or task whose waiver another user approved counts as cleared, at the lock request and at the lock decision; journal balancing, journal completeness and the controller certification cannot be waived. Rev 1.101 (supervisor rulings R-114 (b) and R-116 (e)): the period's close run is a lock gate — the latest close run of the entity, book and period succeeded, and no contract holds a period end of the period, or of an earlier one, that is still to post since; it cannot be waived, because a period end that was not posted is not a risk a signature accepts, and the remedy is to run the close again. The server enforces the rule (04 §16.8). Rev 1.202 (item CLO-WAIVER-COVERS-LATER-1; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02 17:32 on the lane's line; 04 T-CLS-03 rev 1.305): A waiver covers the COUNT it was approved for, with the gate's sentence: the request's content (the hash its approver decides on) carries the item's stored count and detail, so a count that moves before the decision makes the request stale by the approval kernel's own rule — 409 `stale-approval`, the request void (NTF-04), the requester asks again. A gate that later counts MORE than its waiver covered fails again, with the count that stands and its sentence followed by "The waiver <request no> covered <m>.", at every evaluation, the lock request and the decision among them: the item leaves `WAIVED` for `FAILED`, names no request until another is asked for, and its stored result keeps the spent waiver's number and count (`waiver_outgrown`); the earlier request stays in the audit trail and in any certification that holds it. A count at or below the waived one stays `WAIVED`, both counts stated as before. The reopen of a period ends the waivers of the close it reopens: the `PERIOD_REOPEN` decision returns every `WAIVED` item of the period to `NOT_STARTED`, in its transaction, and the lock that was reopened keeps what it certified — a waiver accepts what stood before one certification. Limit, stated: a count does not see one item replaced by another; where the rows a gate counts have an identity — exception items — the waiver of the item itself is the exact road, and a waived item stops counting. A waiver approved for an item that stored no count covers the gate as before. A signed close task is not touched by a reopen, and `NOT_APPLICABLE` stays final. Rev 1.206 (item CLO-APPROVALS-WAIVER-OWN-REQUEST-1; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-03): nor is the request that asks to waive the pending-approvals gate a pending approval affecting the period — the request that asks to waive the count is no part of the count (04 §16.8 `blockers` rev 1.318). | REQ-CLS-009, 019, 021 |
| BR-CLS-02 | A period is submitted for lock by one user and locked by another holder of `period.lock`. | T-REF-07 |
| BR-CLS-03 | Locking a period moves the next period from `future` to `open` if needed, so late events always have a first open period. The contracts with an effect in the period so opened are re-marked for recomputation by a job the lock defers (04 E-14 `PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY`); until that job has succeeded the opened period's gate "All contracts computed" fails with "Contracts not re-marked since the period was opened" and cannot be waived, so the period is not locked before its scheduled revenue can post (rev 1.69; supervisor rulings R-101 (a), R-106 (a)). A lock of a period of the primary book opens the next period of the LEGACY book with it, where the entity keeps that book and its periods up to the locked one are open: the LEGACY book follows the close of the primary book and has no lock of its own to open it, and a legacy amount is posted only in a period open in both books (rev 1.143; 04 §14.1 rev 1.214). | D-19 |
| BR-CLS-04 | In `closing`, only holders of `period.lock` may submit manual adjustments for the period; imports follow BR-DAT-07. The submit needs `period.lock` for the entity in place of `adjustment.create`; preparing a draft (create, edit, preview, attach, discard) stays with `adjustment.create`, and withdrawing a request or asking to defer an adjustment past lock, which post nothing, takes either (rev 1.49). | REQ-CLS-003 |
| BR-CLS-05 | A period can be reopened only when no later period of the same entity and book is `closed` or `permanently_locked` (ERR-16). | [J] Keeps opening balances of later periods consistent |
| BR-CLS-06 | In a `reopened` period, every posting command (import, manual event, manual adjustment, journal run) needs a human approval by a user other than the submitter; auto-approval rules do not apply; subledger lines carry `is_post_reopen = true` (T-SL-04). A soft close does not end the rule: ended, it returns the period to `reopened` (SM-07; rev 1.99). Nor does a soft close suspend it (rev 1.113): the rule and the flag hold for a period that was locked once and is not locked now — its current lock record is the `REOPEN` — so a line posted during the soft close that follows a reopen, where the close run posts its period-end entries and adjustments are submitted, is a post-reopen line. | REQ-CLS-011 (SoD-6) |
| BR-CLS-07 | Re-lock generates the diff report against the previous lock snapshot automatically and adds it to the evidence pack. | REQ-CLS-011 |
| BR-CLS-08 | A period is submitted for lock, and locked, only when no earlier period of the same entity and book is postable (`open`, `closing` or `reopened`) (ERR-65). The rule holds at the request and again at the decision. An earlier `future` period does not block: nothing posts into it and periods open in order (SM-07). The permanent-lock order (SM-07) and BR-CLS-05 are unchanged. | [J] Supervisor ruling R-6 (CLO-LOCK-ORDER-1): a posting into an earlier postable period would move the opening balance of a locked period after its figures were frozen; standard period-close control, not a GAAP conclusion |
| BR-JE-01 | A journal run is approved by a user other than the runner. Rev 1.33: in person or through a delegate — neither the approver nor the delegator on whose behalf a decision is taken is the runner (REQ-PLT-011), at every step of the request. | REQ-JE-004, REQ-PLT-011 |
| BR-JE-02 | Repeating an export command returns the existing export records; the adapter never posts a chunk twice. | REQ-JE-012, 013 |
| BR-JE-03 | For the CSV target, "Record ERP reference" acknowledges a batch with the ERP document reference and user. | REQ-JE-016 |
| BR-JE-04 | What a batch states when it leaves eRev (rev 1.82; supervisor ruling R-110, pending the independent accountant as candidate AD-55): the CSV file states each line in the transaction currency and in the entity's functional currency; a batch sent to NetSuite or QuickBooks Online is stated in the entity's functional currency with the functional amounts, the transaction currency and amounts for information. The ledger's accounts that eRev posts to carry these balances in the functional currency only, and the ledger does not revalue them: eRev's foreign-currency remeasurement is the only one. | REQ-JE-011, 012, 014, 015 |
| BR-RPT-01 | Every report view, export and pack generation creates a report run record; the "Run details" drawer shows it. | REQ-RPT-002 |
| BR-RPT-02 | Reports for a `closed` or `permanently_locked` period default to "As locked"; users may switch to "Current" with a visible label. | REQ-CLS-010 |
| BR-RPT-03 | Sections suppressed by nonpublic elections show "Omitted under the <election> election." | REQ-RPT-025 |
| BR-FC-01 | A scenario tenant copies the source tenant's memberships as of the snapshot; the creator is added with Viewer and `scenario.use` if not already a member. | REQ-FC-001 |
| BR-FC-02 | In a scenario tenant, forecast event sets and what-if modifications apply without approval routing and are audited. | REQ-FC-002, 007 |
| BR-FC-03 | A scenario tenant cannot be converted, cannot export journals and cannot write to production (ERR-17, ERR-26). It shreds no file it shares (rev 1.156): the files of a sandbox copy are stored once, for the copy and the workspace it was copied from, so the shredding of such a file, the request for it and the approval of such a request are refused in the sandbox (403 `sandbox-restricted`: "This file belongs to the workspace this sandbox was copied from and cannot be shredded here."); a file uploaded into the sandbox itself is the sandbox's own and is shredded there. | REQ-PLT-022 |
| BR-AI-01 | Proposed fields with confidence below 0.75 carry the chip "Check"; low-confidence fields are never pre-selected for acceptance. | REQ-AI-005 |
| BR-AI-02 | Enabling AI, changing provider or model, or enabling contract-text sending is a configuration change approved by another Tenant Admin. | REQ-AI-002 |
| BR-AI-03 | "Turn off AI now" applies immediately without approval and is audited. | REQ-AI-010 |
| BR-AI-04 | Proposals expire after 30 days without action. | [J] Stale extractions should not be accepted |
| BR-AI-05 | Accepting a proposal runs the normal command as the accepting user; drafts created from proposals carry flag `AI_ASSISTED`, which routes activation to a Revenue Reviewer regardless of amount. | REQ-AI-003; REQ-PLT-013 |
| BR-MIG-01 | A legacy database is read from a copy; its SHA-256 is stored; a repeated file is refused (ERR-19). Rev 1.170: a migration is an act on the workspace — the legacy database holds the contracts of any entity and the import creates entities — so it is created, read, profiled, imported, reconciled and cancelled by a member who holds the permission to run migrations for all entities; a member whose role names entities is refused (403), as at the workspace's other acts (BR-UX-06). | REQ-MIG-004; REQ-PLT-012 |
| BR-MIG-02 | Promotion requires an approved reconciliation whose unexplained differences are 0; differences with a DEVIATIONS reference are allowed. | REQ-MIG-003 |
| BR-MIG-03 | Replay batches commit in the replay sandbox without per-batch approval; production re-execution records the promotion approval id on every batch. | REQ-MIG-002 |
| BR-MIG-04 | Migrated history rows display "Migrated, unattributed" and are excluded from audit-trail views. | REQ-MIG-001 |
| BR-INT-01 | Connection settings store credential reference names, never values; test connection records result and UTC time. Rev 1.170: a connection belongs to the entities it serves. It is listed for, read by and changed, tested and synced by an administrator whose integration permission covers every one of them — an administrator of all entities when it serves every entity — and to anyone else it is "not found", with its sync runs and its external ids; nobody points a connection at an entity outside their own scope (BR-UX-06). | REQ-INT-006; REQ-PLT-012 |
| BR-INT-02 | Ingestion applies source objects in source-version order; an older version arriving later is recorded and ignored. | REQ-INT-001 |
| BR-INT-03 | A control-total mismatch fails the batch, raises an exception, notifies the integration owner and blocks lock. | REQ-DAT-010 |

### 5.4 Notifications

Rules: NTF-R1 identical notifications (kind, subject) within 10 minutes are merged. NTF-R2 email goes through the SMTP adapter (fake in tests); users set per-kind preferences, except NTF-09, whose in-app and email delivery cannot be turned off (04 T-PLT-25 `ck_notification_preference__mandatory`). In a sandbox workspace no email is sent: a notification raised there is delivered in the app only, NTF-09 included (05 SBX-08; rev 1.106). NTF-R3 emails contain the object reference and a link, never amounts or customer data [J: emails leave the controlled environment]; in-app items may show amounts. NTF-R4 in-app times are relative with the absolute UTC time in the tooltip (DS-FMT-18). NTF-R5 this table is the authority for triggers, recipients, email defaults and the mandatory kind; 04 T-PLT-25 seeds the email defaults from it and `docs/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.8 defers to it (B1-013). NTF-R6 the Link column names the target SF id; `notification.link_path` (T-PLT-24) holds the route that SCREENS assigns to that SF id (D-73).

| Id | Kind (E-69) | Trigger | Recipients | Title | Body (in-app) | Link | Email default |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NTF-01 | `APPROVAL_ASSIGNED` | A step becomes active | Holders of the step permission in scope, excluding the preparer and earlier approvers; active delegates | "Approval needed: <summary>" | "<preparer> submitted <object> for approval. Impact: <amount with code>." | SF-12 request detail | on |
| NTF-02 | `ITEM_APPROVED` (E-69 rev 1.1) | Request approved | Preparer | "Approved: <summary>" | "<approver> approved <object>." | subject (its SF surface) | off |
| NTF-03 | `ITEM_REJECTED` | Request rejected | Preparer | "Rejected: <summary>" | "<approver> rejected <object>: <comment>" | subject (its SF surface) | on |
| NTF-04 | `APPROVAL_VOIDED` | Request voided or withdrawn | Preparer and assigned approvers | "Approval voided: <summary>" | "The item changed after submission, so the approval request was voided. Resubmit to route it again." | subject (its SF surface) | on |
| NTF-05 | `JOB_FAILED` | Job `FAILED` (E-13) or dead-lettered | Job initiator; Controllers of the entity for close runs; the uploader of an import for its dry run, a job the system starts (rev 1.186: the step reads "its last attempt") | "Job failed: <job label>" | "<job label> failed at <step>: <reason>. Nothing was committed." For a job that was stopped after ten minutes without a heartbeat (rev 1.191; ERR-49): "<job label> failed at <step>: no heartbeat for 10 minutes. The job was stopped; what it had not committed by then is not kept." | originating SF surface of the job | on |
| NTF-06 | `PERIOD_LOCKED` (E-69 rev 1.1) | Period locked | Controllers, Revenue Reviewers and Auditors with the entity in scope | "<entity> <period> locked" | "<user> locked <period> for <entity> in book <book>. The evidence pack is available." | SF-05 cockpit | off |
| NTF-07 | `PERIOD_REOPENED` (E-69 rev 1.1) | Period reopened | Controllers, Revenue Reviewers and Auditors with the entity in scope | "<entity> <period> reopened" | "<requester> reopened <period>: <reason>. Approved by <approver 1> and <approver 2>." | SF-05 cockpit | on |
| NTF-08 | `CLOSE_BLOCKER_RAISED` | A new blocker for a `closing` period | Blocker owner; Controllers | "Close blocker: <entity> <period>" | "<blocker label> (<count>)." | SF-05 cockpit | off |
| NTF-09 | `CHAIN_VERIFICATION_FAILED` | Verification fails | Tenant Admins and Controllers who hold the role for all entities. Rev 1.175: the audit chain is the workspace's own, so a holder of either role for named entities is not told. A workspace in which no member holds either role for all entities has no reader of this notice; the platform operator's alert of the same failure (05 OPR-24) is raised all the same and names the workspace, the verification and the first failing event, and the operator follows runbook RB-08 | "Audit chain verification failed" | "Verification stopped at event <n>. Open the verification details and follow the runbook." | SF-09 audit log verification | on (mandatory) |
| NTF-10 | `EXPORT_FAILED` | A journal batch or run becomes `failed` (E-34), or a chunk is rejected | Exporter; Controllers whose role covers the batch's entity (rev 1.175: a Controller of other entities alone is not told) | "Journal export failed: <run>" | "<adapter> rejected <n> chunks: <reason>. Retry after correcting the cause; retries do not duplicate postings." Rev 1.133 (item JRN-CANCEL-APPROVED-PENDING-1; the supervisor's answer of 2026-10-01): when the dispatch ended with an error of eRev's own and not with the ledger's refusal (05 ADP-31), the body does not say that the ledger rejected a chunk — "eRev could not complete the export through <adapter> (<slug or class>); the ledger may hold the batch. Retry it: a retry asks the ledger first, so nothing posts twice." | SF-06 run detail | on |
| NTF-11 | `EXCEPTION_ASSIGNED` | Exception created or assigned | Owner; integration owner for sync and interface items; for an item `FX_RATE_CHANGED_AFTER_LOCK` the Revenue Accountants and the Controllers whose roles cover its entity (rev 1.197: a Controller certified the lock the rate changed after) | "Exception: <code>" | "<message>" (IMP copy of the code) | SF-11 | off |
| NTF-12 | `SUPPORT_GRANT_REQUESTED` | Operator requests support access | Tenant Admins who hold the role for all entities, the requester apart. Rev 1.175: an operator's access reaches every entity, and the request is decided by a Tenant Admin of all entities (BR-UX-06); a Tenant Admin of named entities is neither told nor asked. The bootstrap Tenant Admin holds the role for all entities, so the notice has a reader while that grant stands | "Support access requested" | "<operator> requests <scope> access from <start> to <end> UTC." | SF-14 support access | on |

### 5.5 User-facing copy and error messages

Copy cells of the ERR and IMP tables below are CommonMark-escaped text: a literal pipe is written `\|`, an inner quote may be written `\"`, and every reader (the DG-ARC-13 copy-catalogue guard and any parsed-copy check) splits a row on unescaped pipes only and unescapes exactly `\|` and `\"` before counting cells or comparing copy with code; no other backslash is admitted in a copy cell (rev 1.19, COPY-ESCAPE-1).

Tone guide (extends DS-BR-10 to 12 and DS-CPY-01 to 08):

| Id | Rule | Example |
|---|---|---|
| CPY-01 | Say what happened in accounting terms, then what to do next. | "Estimated total costs (500,000.00) cannot be lower than costs incurred to date (502,000.00)." |
| CPY-02 | Name objects by business id (external id, obligation key, product code, period label, entity code). Outside grids, amounts carry the ISO code (DS-FMT-05). | "NS-SO-DE-5004, obligation O1 (AVM-GW)" |
| CPY-03 | Never blame and never say "invalid" without the rule that would be valid. | "Use YYYY-MM-DD or an Excel date." |
| CPY-04 | Permission messages name the missing permission in plain words and who can help. | "You need the lock permission. Ask a controller." |
| CPY-05 | Unexpected errors state that nothing was saved and give a reference; never stack traces, SQL or internal UUIDs. | "The request was not completed and nothing was saved. Reference 7f3c…a2e1." |
| CPY-06 | Import findings start with the location: "<Sheet> row <n>, column <name>:" (CSV: "Row <n>, column <name>:"), then the message and the code in parentheses. | "Row 2, column Contract: Contract SF-ORD-99999 does not exist in this workspace. (CONTRACT_NOT_FOUND)" |
| CPY-07 | API problem `title` is stable per slug (04 §15.2 rule 2); `detail` equals the UI message; `type` is `https://erev.dev/problems/<slug>` (04 API-C-05), an identifier that is never fetched. An unhandled error returns 500 with `type = "about:blank"` and no slug (ERR-34). A database that could not serve the request at that moment answers 503 with the same `type` and a `Retry-After` header (04 API-C-05 rev 1.114); the interface shows the ERR-64 text for it (rev 1.73; supervisor ruling R-108 (b) (3): it showed the ERR-34 text, whose "nothing was saved" is not true of a lost connection that hid a successful commit). | |
| CPY-08 | Codification citations follow DS-CPY-06; AI copy follows DS-CPY-08. | |

Problem responses (REQ-PLT-028). Slugs and statuses are 04 §15.2 verbatim (D-73); every catalogue slug has one ERR row, and the synonyms of 04 table 15.2-S are never emitted. Where a convention is violated without a dedicated slug, the response is 422 `validation-failed` with the convention id in `errors[].rule_id`, and the ERR row gives the `errors[].message` copy.

The latest figure is not answered for an earlier period." — a period balance the contract version's trace cannot answer (ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-07a): a report run ends `FAILED` with the Problem, the dataset freeze of a lock refuses and the dashboard read answers it (rev 1.42; supervisor rulings R-16 and R-42 (b)) |

| ERR-60 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "S15-R-18c"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `detail` "<period key> of <entity> in book <book> is not locked: <reason>" for one finding and "<period key> of <entity> in book <book> is not locked: <count> findings; the first: <reason>" for several; one `errors[]` entry per finding, `errors[].message` "<count> subledger line(s) of the period are recorded after the freeze cutoff <cutoff> (the latest at <latest>); the frozen datasets would not hold them.", "<count> journal run(s) of the period are created after the freeze cutoff <cutoff> (the latest at <latest>); the frozen journal population would not hold them.", "contract <external id> has subledger lines of the period within the freeze cutoff <cutoff>, and the contract version that posted them is known only at <known at>; the version-based datasets would not hold the contract.", "subledger lines were posted into the period while the lock was being decided (<before> at the freeze, <after> now); the frozen datasets do not hold them all." or "the journal runs of the period changed while the lock was being decided (<before> at the freeze, <after> now); the frozen journal population is not theirs." — the freeze cutoff of a lock decision does not cover the period (ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-18c): nothing is written, the lock request stays pending and the period in soft close; the request is decided again (rev 1.42; supervisor rulings R-19 and R-42 (b)) |

Try again in a moment. If you were saving a change, check first whether it was saved. Reference <request id>." Shown for a slug-less 503: a database that could not serve the request at that moment, or a connection pool that gave no connection in time (rev 1.73; supervisor ruling R-108 (b) (3)) |

The job ends `FAILED` with this problem and no run is written: journals are never sent to a ledger picked by sort order. `detail` and `errors[].message` "<entity> has more than one active general ledger connection (<codes>). Disable all but one before calculating journals." |
| ERR-68 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "S14-R-21"` on `errors[].field = "entity_code"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | The calculation of a journal run when the entity's general ledger connection states a `config.max_lines_per_chunk` that is not a whole number of at least 2 — a balanced entry has two lines (05 ADP-11; rev 1.74; BUILD_SPEC CLO-15). The job ends `FAILED` and no run is written. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Connection <code> sets max_lines_per_chunk to <value>; it must be a whole number of at least 2." |
| ERR-69 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "S14-R-21"` on `errors[].field = "grain"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | The calculation of a journal run for a NetSuite or QuickBooks Online connection when a journal entry is larger than a chunk and cannot be cut by contract (ENGINE_SPEC_B S14-R-21; 05 ADP-11; rev 1.74; BUILD_SPEC CLO-15). The job ends `FAILED` and no run is written; raise the connection's `max_lines_per_chunk`. The reason is "the part of contract <contract> alone has <part> lines" or "its lines do not balance contract by contract". `detail` and `errors[].message` "A journal entry of <lines> lines cannot be split into chunks of at most <limit> lines: <reason>." |
| ERR-70 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-10"` on `errors[].field = "event_type"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /events/{id}/request-void` on a `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_APPLIED` event: approved, the void would take the adjustment back at the next computation while its T-SL-05 row stayed `POSTED` (SM-10; rev 1.87; supervisor ruling R-112 (b) of 2026-09-30). Nothing is stored or routed. `detail` and `errors[].message` "A posted manual adjustment cannot be voided. Correct it with a further adjustment." |
| ERR-71 | `forbidden` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-10"` on `errors[].field = "created_by"` | 403 | Permission denied | `PATCH /manual-adjustments/{id}` by anyone but the person who created the adjustment, as a draft or after a rejection, answered before the `If-Match` is compared (SM-10; rev 1.87; supervisor ruling R-112 (b) of 2026-09-30). `detail` and `errors[].message` "Only <creator> can change this adjustment." |
| ERR-72 | `lock-conflict` with `errors[].rule_id = "PERIOD_STATE_MOVED"` | 409 | Another change was in progress | A command that records an event and computes in the same transaction — `POST /contracts/{id}/events` where it appends without an approval; the rule holds for every transaction that does both — when a period of the contract's entities and books was locked after the command began and before its computation wrote: the lock froze its datasets without the event, at a cutoff the event's stamp — the start of the command's transaction — precedes. The computation reads the lock records once it holds the period rows it shares with a lock decision; nothing is written, and the refusal is not kept for the request's `Idempotency-Key` — sent again as it is, the command is recorded after the lock and is a late event in every reader (04 §14.1, the note on a command recorded before a lock, rev 1.157; rev 1.86; supervisor rulings R-105 (3) and of 2026-10-01). An approval whose hook records and computes meets the same check where the lock is of an entity its request does not name — one that performs in the contract without contracting it: the decision is rolled back, the request stays pending, and the sentence ends "Decide again: it is then recorded after the lock." Rev 1.187 (supervisor ruling R-122 (j); item PIN-WINDOW-APPENDER-1; 04 §14.1 rev 1.229): a transaction that records an event and stores no computation of it asks the same at its end, for what it recorded on a contract that is not a draft — a command whose computation is deferred, or is stored as quarantined or failed; the commit of an import, after its plans; a document of an adapter's run; an approved SSP override. The commit of an import is run once more in its job, stamped after the lock, and a second refusal ends the import `FAILED` with this sentence before its reference; a sync run applies the objects it fetched once more likewise, and a second refusal ends the run `FAILED` with this problem and its checkpoint unmoved, so that the next run brings the same objects (05 §5.6 rev 1.195). `detail` and `errors[].message` "A period of this contract was locked while the command ran. Nothing was saved. Send it again: it is then recorded after the lock." |
| ERR-73 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "E-34"` on `errors[].field = "state"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | The refusal a `JOURNAL_EXPORT` job names in `result.refusal` when the ledger it asked holds a failed batch (04 §16.7 `cancel` and `hand-over`; rev 1.88; item JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1): the batch gets its `DUPLICATE` receipt and is acknowledged; a run is not cancelled, and a batch is not handed over — then the sentence ends "… and is not handed over." `detail` and `errors[].message` "<ledger> holds batch <external id> as <document>. The batch is acknowledged and journal run <run no> is not cancelled." |
| ERR-74 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "E-34"` on `errors[].field = "state"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /journal-runs/{id}/cancel` on a run with a `failed` batch one of whose other batches is `exported` or `acknowledged` — in a ledger, or handed over as a file: a recalculation would send those lines again under new external ids (rev 1.88; item JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1). Nothing is changed and no job is started. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Journal run <run no> has a batch that a ledger holds or that was handed out (<external id>). Retry its failed batch, or hand it over for manual posting." |
| ERR-75 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "REQ-POL-007"` on `errors[].field = "effective_from"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | The `submit` command of a configuration version that supersedes a published version of its scope, and the approval that publishes it (03 REQ-POL-007; 04 §16.5; rev 1.89; supervisor ruling R-113 (b) of 2026-09-30): the version would take effect where a computation or an act has already chosen. At the approval nothing of the decision is kept and the request stays pending. The date form — an obligation template version, or a version of a `POB_ASSIGNMENT` or `SSP_ASSIGNMENT` rule set, without an effective date, or with one whose UTC date is today or earlier in the time zone of any active legal entity: `detail` and `errors[].message` "This version replaces a published one. Choose an effective date later than today." The instant form — a policy version, or a version of any other rule set, with an effective time earlier than the instant of the check: `detail` and `errors[].message` "This version replaces a published one. Choose an effective time that has not passed." A first version of its template, rule set or policy scope keeps a free date. **Before the first legal entity (rev 1.178; item PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1; supervisor ruling of 2026-10-01).** While the workspace has no legal entity nothing has been computed, so no computation and no act on a legal entity's data can have chosen. A policy version of the categories `ACCOUNTING_POLICY` and `PRACTICAL_EXPEDIENT` that supersedes a published one may then carry an effective time that has passed, and the period-start rule of a period-scoped version does not apply to it: a tenant that migrates dates its policies at the first day of its history. From the first legal entity on the instant form stands as before, also for a version submitted before it — the approval judges the time again and refuses it, and the request stays pending. The other policy categories hold parameters that acts read before any legal entity exists and keep the instant form, as do the rule sets and the date form. |
| ERR-76 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "LEGACY_FOLLOWS_PRIMARY"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /periods/{id}/start-close`, `request-lock`, `request-reopen` and `request-permanent-lock` of a period of the LEGACY book, whatever its state: the LEGACY book has no close of its own and follows the close of the tenant's primary book, named by its label (04 T-REF-06 and §16.8 rev 1.155; rev 1.84; supervisor rulings R-112 (e) and R-114 (d)); nothing is written. Rev 1.160 (item CLO-RUN-LEGACY-1; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-01; 04 rev 1.230): a close run is a command of a close, so the same answer is given by `POST /close-runs` with `book` `LEGACY`, by `POST /close-runs/{id}/resume` of a run of the LEGACY book and — as the `problem` of each entity's result — by `POST /close-runs/multi-entity` with that book; no run, job, close-run number or audit event is written. A caller without `period.close` for the entity is answered as for any book, before the book's rule is told. `cancel` of a run stays available. `detail` and `errors[].message` "The legacy book follows the close of <primary book>." |
| ERR-77 | `precondition-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "RETENTION_UNSET"` | 412 | Record changed | The `problem` of a `TENANT_SNAPSHOT` job whose workspace has no snapshot retention policy in force at the snapshot's `known_at` (04 T-PLT-31 `platform.snapshot_retention_families`); `detail` and `errors[].message` carry the same sentence. With a confirmed policy that is not in force yet: "Sandbox copies are possible from <DD MMM YYYY HH:mm UTC>: the snapshot retention policy approved on <DD MMM YYYY> takes effect then." With none confirmed: "No snapshot retention policy is confirmed yet. A Tenant Admin sets the retention families and a second person approves them; sandbox copies are possible from the time the approved policy takes effect." The request itself is accepted (202): the copy is the `detail` of the failed job, which SF-15:sandbox shows under the failed copy (SCREENS_B §9.7). It names no screen: none sets the retention families yet (item SBX-RETENTION-UI-1) |

It cannot be downloaded." |
| ERR-80 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "REGISTRY_EFFECTIVE_ORDER"` on `errors[].field = "effective_from"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | The `submit` command of a policy version dated at or before the effective instant of the published version of its category and scope (04 T-PLT-32 and §16.5 "Order of effective instants", rev 1.183; rev 1.112; supervisor ruling R-117 (b) of 2026-09-30): effective instants are ordered, so a version never takes effect before the version it stands on, nor together with one that is dated. A published version that names no date is in effect from its own publication instant, and a version dated at that very instant, or submitted at it without a date, is not refused (04 §16.5, the tie; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-01). **Before the first legal entity (rev 1.178; item PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02).** While the workspace has no legal entity, a policy version of the categories `ACCOUNTING_POLICY` and `PRACTICAL_EXPEDIENT` may be dated before the publication instant of the published version of its scope when that version names no date: that version — the default provisioning writes, the first of its scope — answers for every earlier period (04 T-PLT-32 "The value of a period"), and a tenant that migrates dates its policies at the first day of its history (ERR-75). Against a published version that names a date the refusal stands, before the first legal entity as after it; ERR-81 and the settings categories are unchanged. Nothing is stored or routed and the version stays tested. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Version <n> takes effect on <DD MMM YYYY>. Choose an effective date after it." — the number and the date (UTC) are the published version's. |

Choose an effective date after it, or submit this version once version <n> is in effect." |

One `errors[]` entry per record; `errors[].message` "Judgement record <judgement no> of this modification is waiting for review. Its preparer withdraws the review request first." |

One `errors[]` entry per version; `errors[].message` "Estimate version <element code> v<version no> of this modification is waiting for approval. Its preparer withdraws it first." |

Nothing was saved. Decide again." |

Nothing is written. `<period>` is the period's name and `<entity>` the entity's code. For the cancel the sentence ends "… A journal run of a closed period cannot be cancelled." `detail` and `errors[].message` "<period> is closed for <entity> in book <book>. | ERR-87 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-03"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /modifications/{id}/submit` while an estimate version linked to the modification is draft, waiting for approval, rejected or withdrawn; nothing is written. The versions are approved first: the approval of a version moves the contract's head, which the modification's request pins. The approval of the modification checks the same again and then ends "The modification is approved after it." (04 §16.14 rev 1.210; rev 1.139; item MOD-LINKED-ESTIMATES-1). One `errors[]` entry per version; `errors[].message` "Estimate version <element code> v<version no> of this modification is not approved. It is approved before the modification is submitted." |
| ERR-88 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-04"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /estimate-versions/{id}/submit` of a version whose modification was discarded; nothing is written and the version stays a draft, which its preparer discards (SM-04); the element is changed by a version created outside the modification (04 §16.14 rev 1.210; rev 1.139; item MOD-LINKED-ESTIMATES-1). `errors[].message` "Modification <modification no> of this estimate version was discarded. The version cannot be submitted." |
| ERR-92 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "REGISTRY_BASIS_SUPERSEDED"` on `errors[].field = "status"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | The edit of a rejected or withdrawn policy version — `PATCH /policies/{id}`, which would return it to draft (SM-04) — after another version of its category and scope was published since its last submit (04 T-PLT-32 "Whole value set" and §16.5 "The statement of a version", rev 1.183; rev 1.112; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-01 on the report of item REG-VERSION-WHOLE-SET-1): the version holds the whole value set of that submit, made on a version that is no longer the published one, so what its author meant to change cannot be told from what it would now revert. Nothing is written and the version stays `REJECTED` or `WITHDRAWN`. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Version <n> was published after this version was submitted. Create a new version: it starts from the published values." — the number is the published version's. |
| ERR-93 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-04"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /estimates/{id}/versions`, and the `PATCH /estimate-versions/{id}` that would return a rejected or withdrawn version to draft, while another version of the same element is draft or waiting for approval; nothing is written. One version of an element is prepared at a time: before, a second version could be prepared and sent for approval on the same predecessor. A rejected or withdrawn version is not open — it stays as history beside a new one — and the versions of another element are not counted (04 T-CON-13, §16.14 rev 1.241; rev 1.168; item EST-ONE-OPEN-VERSION-1). The entry names the open version; `errors[].message` "Estimate version <element code> v<version no> is open. One version of an element is prepared at a time: approve or discard it first." |
| ERR-94 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-04"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | The approval of a variable-consideration estimate version whose `CONSTRAINT` judgement record is not reviewed; the decision is not recorded, the request stays pending and the version submitted. The record is reviewed first: the approval posts the catch-up, and a review that then rejected the record would leave posted revenue on a refused judgement (03 REQ-POL-008, CTL-049; 04 §16.14 rev 1.241; rev 1.168). The approval refuses in the same way — one entry, the same rule id — a version whose evidence was voided after its submission, with "The evidence of this estimate version is no longer attached. Attach it before the version is approved.", and a version whose record no longer is the `CONSTRAINT` record of its element, in the words of IMP-140. `errors[].message` "Judgement record <judgement no> of this estimate version is not reviewed. It is reviewed before the version is approved." |
| ERR-95 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "SM-03"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /modifications/{id}/submit` while a judgement record whose subject is the modification is draft or waiting for review; nothing is written. The records are reviewed, or discarded, first: a record's review request pins the contract's head, which the applied modification moves, so a record that waits could never be reviewed afterwards. A rejected, a superseded and a discarded record hold nothing; a departure from the proposed treatment whose record is not reviewed still answers ERR-54. The approval of the modification checks the same again for a record added since the submission and then ends "The modification is approved after it." (04 §16.14 rev 1.242; rev 1.169; item MOD-LINKED-JUDGEMENTS-1). One `errors[]` entry per record; `errors[].message` "Judgement record <judgement no> of this modification is not reviewed. It is reviewed, or discarded, before the modification is submitted." |
| ERR-96 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "RUN_NOTHING_PENDING"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `POST /journal-runs` — and its `JOURNAL_RUN_CALCULATE` job, which asks again under the coverage key of the entity, book and period — when a journal run of that entity, book and period that is not cancelled stands and the run asked for would hold no line: a further run without lines would stand after the run it follows and keep it from being cancelled (04 §16.7 API-S-JournalRunCreate rev 1.248; rev 1.174; item JRN-EMPTY-RUN-1). No run is written — the job, where it is the one that refuses, ends `FAILED` and leaves the `INFO` item of a failed job (IMP-137); inside a close run the step calculates nothing and succeeds. The copy says what holds in every such case and does not say that the period is journalised — the lock gate journal completeness says that (BR-CLS-01): a posting of a contract under a journal-export hold is left out, as a run leaves it out — whether it lies beyond the runs or inside the range of the run that left it out. Once the hold is released the next run holds it: since rev 1.184 it takes over what an earlier run left out (item JRN-HELD-AFTER-EXPORT-1; 04 T-SL-06 rev 1.267), where until then no later run reached such a posting (04 §16.7 lists the four cases and the operator's way in each). `<run>` is the number of the latest such run, `<period>` the period's name and `<entity>` the entity's code. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Journal run <run> is the latest run of <period> for <entity> in book <book>, and there is nothing for a new run to summarize." |
| ERR-97 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "CALENDARS_DIFFER"` on `errors[].field = "parameters.entity_codes"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | A report run over entities that keep more than one fiscal calendar, when a parameter names a period by key (`period_key`, `from_period_key`, `to_period_key`, `origin_period_key`) or the report's columns are period keys (the revenue waterfall and the disaggregation, with or without a key) — at `POST /report-runs` and at `POST /report-runs/{id}/rerun` (04 T-RPT-01 rule 6 and §16.9, rev 1.264; rev 1.180; the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-02, item RPT-PERIOD-KEY-CALENDARS-1): a period key is read in each entity's own calendar, so one key can state different months for the entities, and a column per period key sets one month into a column per calendar. No run is stored and nothing is computed. `detail` and `errors[].message` "The entities of this run keep different fiscal calendars, so a period key can name different months for them. Run the report for entities of one calendar." Any other run that names no period by key is not refused: each entity is read at its own period holding the run's date. |
| ERR-98 | `lock-conflict` with `errors[].rule_id = "PERIOD_LOCK_IN_FLIGHT"` | 409 | Another change was in progress | A command, an approval's decision or an import's commit that has posted to the ledger and then reads the period rows it shares with a lock decision — the rows of the periods a close run has taken up — while such a decision holds one of them: with the ledger's chain head held it does not wait, since every posting of the book in the workspace would queue behind it, and ends at once; nothing was waited for and nothing is saved (04 §14.1 DB-07 rev 1.229; dev-guide DG-KRN-DB-08 (1c) rev 1.218; rev 1.187; finding F4 of the independent review of 2026-10-01; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02). The commands that post before they compute take those rows first and wait there with no head held; this is what a transaction that reaches the read under a head is answered. `detail` and `errors[].message` "A period of this contract is being locked. Nothing was saved. Send it again." |
| ERR-101 | `invalid-transition` with `errors[].rule_id = "RUN_RECORD_UNREADABLE"` | 409 | Action not available in this state | `GET /journal-lines/{id}/drill` of a journal line of a run whose record of the lines it left out as held cannot be read: its `CALCULATE` event names no ids and its held detail file is missing, shredded or does not verify — only a run calculated before the event recorded the ids can be in that case. Such a run journalises nothing: the lock gate journal completeness names it (BR-CLS-01), the lines of its range answer `journal_run_id` null, and the source lines of its journal lines are not named by a guess (04 T-SL-06 drill-back rev 1.288; rev 1.195; item SUBLEDGER-LINE-JOURNAL-RUN-1). Until that revision the drill decided by the timestamps of the holds and answered lines for every run. A `draft` or `approved` run is cancelled, the latest run of its period first, and the period calculated again; for an `exported`, `acknowledged` or `failed` run the held detail file is restored from a backup. `<run>` is the run's number. `detail` and `errors[].message` "Journal run <run> cannot be traced to its source lines: the record of the lines it left out cannot be read." |
| ERR-102 | `policy-level-not-allowed` with `errors[].rule_id = "POLICY_OVERRIDE_NOT_OFFERED"` | 422 | Policy level not allowed | `POST /policy-overrides`, every creation (rev 1.209; 04 T-CON-23 "Not offered in release 1.0" rev 1.322; item POLICY-OVERRIDE-WITHDRAW-1; supervisor ruling R-126 (b) (4) and (c)): no computation reads a policy override, so none is created in release 1.0 — the request is answered after the route's 403 and the contract's 404 and before anything it names is validated, whatever it names, and nothing is stored. `detail` and `errors[].message`, two sentences: "Policy overrides for a contract or an obligation are not offered in this release." and what decides the named parameter instead — POLICIES §0.5 table 0.5-A for a parameter that lists level C or O, for example "It is set on the product or on its obligation template."; for any other, "It can be set only at <levels> level.", "The framework fixes this value.", "It can be set at no level of the policy registry." or, for a key the registry does not hold, "The policy registry holds no parameter of this key." |
| ERR-103 | `validation-failed` with `errors[].rule_id = "POLICY_PRODUCT_LEVEL_NOT_READ"` on `errors[].field = "policy_values.<key>"` | 422 | Check the highlighted fields | The policy values of a product — `POST /products`, `PATCH /products/{id}` and `POST /products/{id}/propose-policy-values-change` — and of an obligation template version — `POST /pob-templates/{id}/versions` and `PATCH /pob-template-versions/{id}` — that state a value no computation reads (04 T-REF-20 and T-REF-23, rev 1.323; POLICIES §0.5 rule 1, rev 1.125; rev 1.210; supervisor ruling R-126 (c) and the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-03, item PRODUCT-POLICY-VALUE-NOT-READ-1): the engine reads three parameters that list the product level for a contract, and a product's or a template's value is given to it for an obligation, so the value would be stored, approved with the template's version and used by no computation. One error for each such key; nothing is stored and no approval request is opened. `usage.tier_minimum_method` (POL-240) and `upfront_fee.recognition_period` (POL-029), any value but the framework's default: `errors[].message` "This release reads <key> from no product or template. The framework's default applies to every contract: leave it out, or state the default." `pob.shipping_as_fulfilment` (POL-021), every value: `errors[].message` "This release reads <key> from no product or template. The entity's or the workspace's value applies where the framework does not fix it: set it by a policy version." In both the sentence names the parameter: the key is its code, as the field has it after `policy_values.` — a form that shows the refusal in a banner shows the sentence alone. Every other parameter that lists the product level is stated as before. |

Finding and exception code copy. Codes, severities and conditions are `docs/04-DATA_MODEL.md` §15.4 verbatim (D-73): IMP-01 to IMP-40 are table 15.4-A, which absorbs `docs/legacy/DEVIATIONS.md` §5; IMP-41 to IMP-50 are table 15.4-B; IMP-51 to IMP-59 table 15.4-C; IMP-60 to IMP-75 table 15.4-D; IMP-76 to IMP-81 table 15.4-E; IMP-82 to IMP-86 table 15.4-F; IMP-87 and IMP-88 table 15.4-G. IMP-89 to IMP-96 supply copy for the codes that D-76 adds to 04 §15.4, in the tables 04 assigns: `COMBINATION_SUGGESTED` (§9 Q10), `PRINCIPAL_AGENT_NOT_ASSESSED`, `VC_TARGET_TOLERANCE_EXCEEDED` and `OPENING_BALANCE_INCONSISTENT` (ENGINE_SPEC:OQ-A-11 to OQ-A-13), `BILL_AND_HOLD_CRITERIA_UNMET` and `INVOICE_STATUS_UPDATE_MISMATCH` (ENGINE_SPEC_B:OQ-B-04, OQ-B-08), `TRACE_TOO_LARGE` and `FORMULA_NO_CACHED_VALUE` (05:OQ-ARC-12) (rev 1.2). IMP-97 to IMP-105 supply copy for the rev 1.2 codes that had none: `REASON_CODE_NOT_ALLOWED` and `DEAL_PREVIEW_SAVE_NOT_ALLOWED` (table 15.4-D), and the activation checklist codes `MANDATORY_FIELDS`, `SOURCE_REFERENCE`, `PRODUCT_TEMPLATE_SSP`, `DISTINCT_REVIEW`, `COMBINATION_SUGGESTIONS`, `JUDGEMENT_RECORDS` and `STEP1_RECORD` (table 15.4-I), whose messages equal SCREENS §4.9.8 (rev 1.3). IMP-106 supplies `MOD_UNIT_HISTORY_AMBIGUOUS` (04 rev 1.9); IMP-107 supplies `CPC_RELATED_SCOPE_INVALID` (04 rev 1.11; D-91). IMP-117 to IMP-122 supply the `ssp_values` declaration codes of table 15.4-A (04 rev 1.74; D-97 (3), (3a)), whose messages are API-S-SspEntry's verbatim (rev 1.16). IMP-132 supplies `CLOSE_RUN_FAILED` (table 15.4-D; 04 rev 1.134; rev 1.63), the exception item of a failed close run (05 JOB-07); its message is the item's. IMP-137 supplies `JOB_FAILED` (table 15.4-D; 04 rev 1.226; rev 1.157), the exception item of a failed job whose record names one legal entity (05 JOB-07); its title is the title of NTF-05. Every code of 04 §15.4 that is not withdrawn has exactly one IMP row; the withdrawn codes of table 15.4-H have none. The finding severity maps to the exception severity by E-43 (`ERROR` → `BLOCKING`, `WARNING` → `WARNING`, `INFO` → `INFO`). The message is binding UI copy for `import_row` messages, exception items (T-IMP-05 `message`) and `errors[].message`; it is not asserted by the parity suite. Placeholders in angle brackets (rev 1.1; B1-014).

| IMP-139 | `ESTIMATE_ATTESTATION_REASON` | ERROR | "The values equal the approved version: this is an attestation of no change. State its reason in at least 10 characters." |
| IMP-140 | `ESTIMATE_CONSTRAINT_RECORD` | ERROR | "Name the CONSTRAINT judgement record of this element: a record of this contract for <element code>, sent for review or reviewed for this version’s current figures." |
| IMP-141 | `ESTIMATE_ATTESTATION_VALUES` | ERROR | "The values differ from the approved version: this is not an attestation of no change." |
| IMP-143 | `STEP1_RECORD_OVERTAKEN` | ERROR | "The Step 1 assessment of <book> rests on judgement record <judgement no>, which the review of judgement record <later judgement no> has overtaken. Record the assessment on the latest review." |
| IMP-144 | `FX_RATE_CHANGED_AFTER_LOCK` | WARNING | "<period> is <state> for <entity> in book <book>. The approval of version <version no> of rate set <set code> changed <count> exchange <rates> dated in it: <first><more>. <roads> The difference is not computed here. What remains of it to post goes to <posting>. The contracts the changed rates reach are recalculated by the next close run of their contracting entity or by the next change to them: what the rates change in the amounts of their events is posted with the closed period of each event as origin period, and the out-of-period register lists it as Fx republish or with the event that carried it. The close run of <posting> posts what remains of a period-end remeasurement as an amount of that period, without an origin period. Where nothing remains nothing is posted: a closing rate of a period that is followed by another closed period moves an amount between the two for a balance open through both."; `<state>` is "closed" or "permanently locked"; `<rates>` is "rate" for one and "rates" otherwise; `<first>` is the first changed rate by date and pair: "<base>/<quote> <rate type> rate of <on> from <before> to <after>", for a rate that appears "<base>/<quote> <rate type> rate of <on>, <after> where there was none", for a rate that disappears "<base>/<quote> <rate type> rate of <on>, none where it was <before>"; `<more>` is empty for one rate and otherwise ", and <n> more"; `<roads>` is "Reopen the period to restate it, or request a waiver to accept the difference.", while a later period of the entity and book is closed "To restate it, reopen the later closed periods first and then this one; or request a waiver to accept the difference.", and for a permanently locked period or a period behind one "The period cannot be reopened. Request a waiver to accept the difference."; `<posting>` is the key of the period what remains of the difference posts to, or "the next period to open" |
| IMP-145 | `JUDGEMENT_RECORD_REJECTED` | ERROR | "Judgement record <judgement no> (<topic>) was rejected. Discard it, or have its author revise it and send it for review again." |
| IMP-147 | `HEADER_VALUE_CONFLICT` | ERROR | "This row states \"<this value>\" where row <first row> of the same <object> states \"<its value>\". Repeat the value of row <first row> or leave this cell blank."; for a required column, which every row states: "This row states \"<this value>\" where row <first row> of the same <object> states \"<its value>\". Repeat the value of row <first row>."; where the first row leaves the cell blank: "This row states \"<this value>\" where row <first row> of the same <object> leaves the cell blank. State it on row <first row> or leave this cell blank." <object> is what the rows build together — an object, or a line of it — named by its key (CPY-02): "mapping version <name>", "contract <external id>", "estimate <contract> / <element code>", "document <invoice number>", "document line <invoice number> / <line id>", "SSP book version <book code> / <label>", "SSP entry <product code>". The sentence names no header: the code's name is older than the rule's reach to a line's cells. A value longer than 60 characters is quoted by its first 60 and an ellipsis. |
| IMP-148 | `USAGE_PERIOD_NOT_ENDED` | ERROR | "The usage period <start> to <end> ends after the report's date, <date>. A usage report states usage that has occurred: end the period on or before <date>." |

Checks: SoD-1 holds because TA holds only access-administration permissions, `*.read`, `audit.read` and `settings.manage`; SoD-2 to SoD-5 and SoD-7 hold because no default role holds both functions; SoD-6 is enforced per transaction (BR-CLS-06). Roles that hold a `requires_mfa` permission (RR, CO, SP, IA, TA) force MFA enrolment (REQ-PLT-005). RR and CO hold both `period.reopen_request` and `period.reopen_approve`; a requester can never decide their own request (SM-01, `self-approval`), and the `PERIOD_REOPEN` routing requires two approvers other than the requester, at least one holding Controller (§2.5), so a one-controller S1 tenant can reopen with its Controller and a Revenue Reviewer (REQ-CLS-011; D-75 Q12).

Reports and the access registers (rev 1.57; supervisor ruling R-63 (a)). `report.run` runs a report (ACT-35), with three exceptions that hold no accounting data and are run under `audit.read` instead — the permission that already opens the audit log, which carries every grant: the user access listing, the SoD conflict report and the API client inventory (SCREENS_B RPT-24, RPT-25, RPT-27). A Tenant Admin therefore runs them (J-22.8) and so does an Auditor (J-17.6); a Viewer, an SSP Analyst and an SSP Approver do not. The configuration change register and the approvals register (RPT-23, RPT-26) need `report.run` and `audit.read` together, as the audit log export and the chain verification report do (04 API-R-41). A file output needs `report.export` in every case (ACT-36): a Tenant Admin reads the three registers on screen and does not export them; the Auditor and the Controller export.
